To establish Theorem 1 , it is only left for us to control the last term in Eqn. ( 75 ).
First, by Jensen’s inequality, φ ⁡ ( t ) \varphi(t) is lower bounded by
φ ( t ) ≥ − 1 S ∑ i ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ∈ [ S ] log ( 𝔼 x t ∼ q t [ s t ( x t ⊕ i c , x t ) ] ) = 0 . \displaystyle\varphi(t)\geq-\frac{1}{S}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{c\in[S]}\log\big({\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim q_{t}}\left[s_{t}(x_{t}\oplus_{i}c,x_{t})\right]\big)=0.
(76)
For the upper bound, from the definition of φ ⁡ ( t ) \varphi(t) , it satisfies that
φ ( t ) ≤ 1 S 𝔼 x t ∼ q t [ | { y t : d_H ( y t , x t ) = 1 } | ⋅ sup x , y : d_H ⁡ ( x , y ) = 1 | log ( s t ( y , x ) ) | ] , \displaystyle\varphi(t)\leq\frac{1}{S}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim q_{t}}\left[\big|\{y_{t}:\ham(y_{t},x_{t})=1\}\big|\cdot\sup_{x,y:\ham(x,y)=1}\big|\log\left(s_{t}(y,x)\right)\big|\right],
(77)
where | { y t : d_H ⁡ ( y t , x t ) = 1 } | |\{y_{t}:\ham(y_{t},x_{t})=1\}| denotes the cardinality of the set { y t : d_H ⁡ ( y t , x t ) = 1 } \{y_{t}:\ham(y_{t},x_{t})=1\} , which equals d ⁡ ( S − 1 ) d(S-1) for any x t ∈ 𝒳 x_{t}\in{\mathcal{X}} .
It therefore suffices to control the quantity | log ⁡ ( s t ​ ( y t , x t ) ) | |\log\left(s_{t}(y_{t},x_{t})\right)| , which is achieved through the following lemma.
Lemma 10 .
For any distribution q 0 q_{0} on 𝒳 {\mathcal{X}} , let q t q_{t} be the marginal distribution of the uniform noising process at time t t . Then, for any x , y ∈ 𝒳 x,y\in{\mathcal{X}} such that d_H ⁡ ( x , y ) = 1 \ham(x,y)=1 , it holds that
| log ⁡ s t ​ ( y , x ) | ≲ log ⁡ ( S ) + max ⁡ { log ⁡ ( t − 1 ) , 0 } . \displaystyle|\log s_{t}(y,x)|\lesssim\log(S)+\max\{\log(t^{-1}),0\}.
As a consequence of Lemma 10 , we arrive at
φ ( t ) ≤ d ⁡ ( S − 1 ) S ⋅ sup x , y : d_H ⁡ ( x , y ) = 1 | log ( s t ( y , x ) ) | ≲ d ( log ( S ) + max { log ( t − 1 ) , 0 } ) . \displaystyle\varphi(t)\leq\frac{d(S-1)}{S}\cdot\sup_{x,y:\ham(x,y)=1}|\log\left(s_{t}(y,x)\right)|\lesssim d\big(\log(S)+\max\{\log(t^{-1}),0\}\big).
(78)
In addition, we make the observation in Lemma 12 that φ ⁡ ( t ) \varphi(t) is a non-increasing function in t t .
Now we are ready to combine everything and bound the last term of Eqn. ( 75 ).
Define Δ = max k ⁡ { t k + 1 − t k } \Delta=\max_{k}\{t_{k+1}-t_{k}\} , and choose 1 ≤ M ≤ N − 1 1\leq M\leq N-1 such that T − t M ∈ [ Δ , 2 ​ Δ ] T-t_{M}\in[\Delta,2\Delta] .
Armed with Eqns. ( 76 ), ( 78 ) and the monotonicity of φ ⁡ ( t ) \varphi(t) , we obtain
∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 ( φ ⁡ ( T − t ) − φ ⁡ ( T − t k ) ) ​ 𝑑 t \displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\big(\varphi(T-t)-\varphi(T-t_{k})\big)\,\mathrm{d}t
≤ ∫ 0 T − t M φ ⁡ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t + ∑ k = 0 M − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 ( φ ⁡ ( T − t k + 1 ) − φ ⁡ ( T − t k ) ) ​ 𝑑 t \displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{T-t_{M}}\varphi(t)\,\mathrm{d}t+\sum_{k=0}^{M-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\big(\varphi(T-t_{k+1})-\varphi(T-t_{k})\big)\,\mathrm{d}t
≤ 2 ​ Δ ​ d ​ ( log ⁡ ( S ) + log ⁡ ( 2 / Δ ) + 1 ) + Δ ​ ∑ k = 0 N − 2 ( φ ⁡ ( T − t k + 1 ) − φ ⁡ ( T − t k ) ) \displaystyle\leq 2\Delta d\big(\log(S)+\log(2/\Delta)+1\big)+\Delta\sum_{k=0}^{N-2}\big(\varphi(T-t_{k+1})-\varphi(T-t_{k})\big)
≤ 2 ​ Δ ​ d ​ ( log ⁡ ( S ) + log ⁡ ( 2 / Δ ) + 1 ) + Δ ​ φ ​ ( Δ ) ≲ Δ ​ d ​ log ⁡ ( S / Δ ) . \displaystyle\leq 2\Delta d\big(\log(S)+\log(2/\Delta)+1\big)+\Delta\varphi(\Delta)\lesssim\Delta d\log(S/\Delta).
Combining the inequality above with Eqn. ( 75 ) achieves
𝖪𝖫 ( p 0 ∥ q T ) ≤ ε score + e − T d log ( S ) + Δ d log ( S / Δ ) , \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(p_{0}\|q_{T})\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+e^{-T}d\log(S)+\Delta d\log(S/\Delta),
which completes the proof of Theorem 1 .
∎
C.2 Proof of Corollary 1
Choose time horizon T = log ⁡ ( d ​ log ⁡ ( S ) / ε ) T=\log(d\log(S)/\varepsilon) and number of discretization steps
N = Θ ⁡ ( d ​ log ⁡ ( S ) ​ log 3 ⁡ ( d ​ log ⁡ ( S ) / ε ) ε ) = Θ ~ ​ ( d ε ) . \displaystyle N=\Theta\left(\frac{d\log(S)\log^{3}(d\log(S)/\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon}\right)=\widetilde{\Theta}\left(\frac{d}{\varepsilon}\right).
Adopting the upper bound in Theorem 1 leads to
𝖪𝖫 ( q data ∥ p output ) = 𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ p T ) \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(q_{\mathrm{data}}\|p_{\mathrm{output}})=\mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\|p_{T})
≲ ε score + e T ​ d ​ log ⁡ ( S ) + T ​ d N ​ log ⁡ ( S ​ N T ) \displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+e^{T}d\log(S)+\frac{Td}{N}\log\left(\frac{SN}{T}\right)
≲ ε score + ε + ε log ⁡ ( S ) ​ T 2 ​ ( log ⁡ ( S ) + 3 ​ T ) \displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+\varepsilon+\frac{\varepsilon}{\log(S)T^{2}}\Big(\log(S)+3T\Big)
≲ ε score + ε . \displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+\varepsilon.
∎
C.3 Proof of Theorem 2
Recall that the path measures of the backward process and the sampling process
are denoted by Q ​ = 𝑑 ​ { q ← t } t ∈ [ 0 , T − δ ] Q\overset{d}{=}\{\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T-\delta]}
and P ​ = 𝑑 ​ { p t } t ∈ [ 0 , T − δ ] P\overset{d}{=}\{p_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T-\delta]} , respectively.
It can be checked that the path measure Q Q is absolutely continuous with respect to P P . By Girsanov’s theorem for the backward process (e.g. Ren et al. (2025, Corollary 3.4) ), it satisfies
𝖪𝖫 ( Q ∥ P ) \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(Q\,\|\,P)
= 𝖪𝖫 ( q ← 0 ∥ p 0 ) + 1 S ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 𝔼 x t k , x t ∼ q ← t k , t [ ∑ i ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s T − t ( x t ⊕ i c , x t ) \displaystyle=\mathsf{KL}(\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{0}\|p_{0})+\frac{1}{S}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t_{k}},x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t_{k},t}}\bigg[\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{c\in[S]}s_{T-t}(x_{t}\oplus_{i}c,x_{t})
D ( s ^ T − t k ( x t k ⊕ i c , x t k ) , s T − t ( x t ⊕ i c , x t ) ) d t ] . \displaystyle\hskip 200.0003ptD\left(\widehat{s}_{T-t_{k}}(x_{t_{k}}\oplus_{i}c,x_{t_{k}}),s_{T-t}(x_{t}\oplus_{i}c,x_{t})\right)\mathrm{d}t\bigg].
Following the same analysis for Eqn. ( 68 ) in Appendix C.1 , we arrive at
𝖪𝖫 ( Q ∥ P ) \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(Q\,\|\,P)
= ε score + 𝖪𝖫 ( q ← 0 ∥ p 0 ) + ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 ( φ ( T − t ) − φ ( T − t k ) ) d t \displaystyle=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+\mathsf{KL}(\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{0}\|p_{0})+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\left(\varphi(T-t)-\varphi(T-t_{k})\right)\mathrm{d}t
= ε score + 𝖪𝖫 ( q T ∥ p 0 ) + ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 ( φ ( T − t ) − φ ( T − t k ) ) d t , \displaystyle=\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+\mathsf{KL}(q_{T}\|p_{0})+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\left(\varphi(T-t)-\varphi(T-t_{k})\right)\mathrm{d}t,
where the function φ ⁡ ( ⋅ ) \varphi(\cdot) is defined as in Eqn. ( 74 )
φ ( t ) ≔ 1 S 𝔼 x t ∼ q t [ ∑ y t : d_H ⁡ ( y t , x t ) = 1 − log ( s t ( y t , x t ) ) ] . \displaystyle\varphi(t)\coloneqq\frac{1}{S}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim q_{t}}\left[\sum_{y_{t}:\ham(y_{t},x_{t})=1}-\log\left(s_{t}(y_{t},x_{t})\right)\right].
Thus, to achieve 𝖪𝖫 ( Q ∥ P ) ≤ ε score + O ( 1 ) \mathsf{KL}(Q\,\|\,P)\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+O(1) , we need to select N , T N,T and step size schedule such that
𝖪𝖫 ( q T ∥ p 0 ) + ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 ( φ ( T − t ) − φ ( T − t k ) ) d t = O ( 1 ) . \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(q_{T}\|p_{0})+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\left(\varphi(T-t)-\varphi(T-t_{k})\right)\mathrm{d}t=O(1).
(79)
In order to understand the first term in Eqn. ( 79 ),
let us first consider the case when T = 1 T=1 . By the assumption q 0 ∈ 𝒫 γ ​ ( 𝒳 ) q_{0}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\gamma}({\mathcal{X}}) , therefore, we are ensured that
𝖪𝖫 ( q 1 ∥ p 0 ) = ∑ x ∈ 𝒳 q 1 ( x ) log ( q 1 ( x ) ) − ∑ x ∈ 𝒳 q 1 ( x ) log ( p 0 ( x ) ) ≥ γ d log ( S ) ≫ 1 . \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(q_{1}\|p_{0})=\sum_{x\in{\mathcal{X}}}q_{1}(x)\log\big(q_{1}(x))-\sum_{x\in{\mathcal{X}}}q_{1}(x)\log\big(p_{0}(x))\geq\gamma d\log(S)\gg 1.
Hence, it implies that T T must satisfy T > 1 T>1 .
We then focus on the analysis of the second term in Eqn. ( 79 ).
We aim to show that the changing rate, i.e., − φ ′ ​ ( t ) -\varphi^{\prime}(t) , is lower bounded as we come close to the target data distribution (i.e., OPEN t ∈ [ 0 , 1 ] ) t\in[0,1]) , which in turn leads to a lower bound on the difference φ ⁡ ( T − t ) − φ ⁡ ( T − t k ) \varphi(T-t)-\varphi(T-t_{k}) .
We proceed with our analysis under the information-theoretic framework.
For notational convenience, given every i ∈ [ d ] i\in[d] and c ∈ [ S ] c\in[S] , let us define
φ i , c ( t ) = 𝔼 x t ∼ q t [ − log ( s t ( x t ⊕ i c , x t ) ] = 𝔼 x t ∼ q t [ − log ( s t ( N i , c ( x t ) , x t ) ] \displaystyle\varphi_{i,c}(t)=\mathbb{E}_{x_{t}\sim q_{t}}\left[-\log\big(s_{t}(x_{t}\oplus_{i}c,x_{t})\right]=\mathbb{E}_{x_{t}\sim q_{t}}\left[-\log\big(s_{t}(N_{i,c}(x_{t}),x_{t})\right]
(80)
where the operator N i , c : 𝒳 → 𝒳 N_{i,c}:{\mathcal{X}}\to{\mathcal{X}} is defined as N i , c ( x ) = x ⊕ i c N_{i,c}(x)=x\oplus_{i}c . It is easy to check that
φ ⁡ ( t ) = 1 S ​ ∑ i ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ∈ [ S ] φ i , c ​ ( t ) . \varphi(t)=\frac{1}{S}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{c\in[S]}\varphi_{i,c}(t).
Notice that N i , c N_{i,c} is a bijection in 𝒳 {\mathcal{X}} .
We define N i , − c = ( N i , c ) − 1 = N i , S − c N_{i,-c}=(N_{i,c})^{-1}=N_{i,S-c} , where ( N i , c ) − 1 (N_{i,c})^{-1}
is denoted as the inverse function of N i , c N_{i,c} .
Since φ ⁡ ( t ) \varphi(t) can be written as a linear combination of φ i , c ​ ( t ) \varphi_{i,c}(t) , it suffices to study the properties of the individual φ i , c ​ ( t ) \varphi_{i,c}(t) to characterize φ ⁡ ( t ) \varphi(t) .
To begin with, the following lemma provides a characterization of φ ⁡ ( t ) \varphi(t) and φ i , c ​ ( t ) \varphi_{i,c}(t) as information-theoretic quantities.
Lemma 11 .
For φ ⁡ ( t ) \varphi(t) and φ i , c ​ ( t ) \varphi_{i,c}(t) defined in Eqns. ( 74 ) and ( 80 ), we have
φ i , c ​ ( t ) \displaystyle\varphi_{i,c}(t)
= 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ∥ ( N i , − c ) # q t ) ; \displaystyle=\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}\,\|\,(N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t});
φ ⁡ ( t ) \displaystyle\varphi(t)
= − ∂ ∂ t 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ∥ p 0 ) = ∑ i ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ∈ [ S ] 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ∥ ( N i , c ) # q t ) , \displaystyle=-\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}\|p_{0})=\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{c\in[S]}\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}\,\|\,(N_{i,c})_{\#}q_{t}),
where ( N i , c ) # (N_{i,c})_{\#} is denoted as the pushforward measure of q t q_{t} under operator N i , c N_{i,c} .
Here, the pushforward measure gives, for any x ∈ 𝒳 x\in{\mathcal{X}} ,
( N i , − c ) # ​ q t ​ ( x ) = q t ​ ( N i , c ​ ( x ) ) . \displaystyle(N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t}(x)=q_{t}\left(N_{i,c}(x)\right).
(81)
Lemma 11 allows us to write φ i , c ​ ( t ) \varphi_{i,c}(t) as the KL divergence between the marginal forward process and its pushforward under N i , c N_{i,c} . By viewing N i , c N_{i,c} as an information channel, we can show it is in a special family of channels, named S S -ary symmetric channel ( Makur and Polyanskiy, 2018 ) , which satisfies strong data processing inequality. Through this idea, we can prove the following lemma. The details are provided in Section E.6 .
Lemma 12 .
For t ∈ ( 0 , T ] t\in(0,T] , φ i , c ​ ( t ) \varphi_{i,c}(t) is differentiable in t t and it holds that
− φ i , c ′ ​ ( t ) ≥ φ i , c ​ ( t ) . \displaystyle-\varphi_{i,c}^{\prime}(t)\geq\varphi_{i,c}(t).
Consequently, Lemma 12 leads to
− φ ′ ( t ) = − 1 S ∑ i ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ∈ [ S ] φ i ​ c ′ ( t ) ≥ 1 S ∑ i ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ∈ [ S ] φ i ​ c ( t ) = φ ( t ) . \displaystyle-\varphi^{\prime}(t)=-\frac{1}{S}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{c\in[S]}\varphi^{\prime}_{ic}(t)\geq\frac{1}{S}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{c\in[S]}\varphi_{ic}(t)=\varphi(t).
Recall the log-Sobolev inequality in Lemma 7 . We have, for any target distribution q 0 ∈ 𝒫 γ ​ ( 𝒳 ) q_{0}\in{\mathcal{P}}^{\gamma}({\mathcal{X}}) and t ∈ ( 0 , 1 ) t\in(0,1) ,
− φ ′ ( t ) ≥ φ ( t ) ≥ 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ∥ p 0 ) ≥ 𝖪𝖫 ( q 1 ∥ p 0 ) ≥ γ d log ( S ) . \displaystyle-\varphi^{\prime}(t)\geq\varphi(t)\geq\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}\|p_{0})\geq\mathsf{KL}(q_{1}\|p_{0})\geq\gamma d\log(S).
Equipped with the above relation, we are ready to control the second term in Eqn. ( 79 ). By the fundamental theorem of calculus, we obtain
∫ t k t k + 1 ( φ ⁡ ( T − t ) − φ ⁡ ( T − t k ) ) ​ 𝑑 t \displaystyle\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\left(\varphi(T-t)-\varphi(T-t_{k})\right)\mathrm{d}t
= ∫ T − t k + 1 T − t k ∫ T − t T − t k − φ ′ ( τ ) d τ d t \displaystyle=\int_{T-t_{k+1}}^{T-t_{k}}\int_{T-t}^{T-t_{k}}-\varphi^{\prime}(\tau)\mathrm{d}\tau\mathrm{d}t
≳ ∫ T − t k + 1 T − t k ( t − t k ) ​ γ ​ d ​ log ⁡ ( S ) ​ 𝑑 t = 1 2 ​ ( t k + 1 − t k ) 2 ​ γ ​ d ​ log ⁡ ( S ) . \displaystyle\gtrsim\int_{T-t_{k+1}}^{T-t_{k}}(t-t_{k})\gamma d\log(S)\mathrm{d}t=\frac{1}{2}(t_{k+1}-t_{k})^{2}\gamma d\log(S).
Choose M M such that T − t M ∈ [ 1 2 , 1 ] T-t_{M}\in[\frac{1}{2},1] . Such M M exists due to the fact that T > 1 T>1 and max k ⁡ { t k + 1 − t k } ≤ 1 2 \max_{k}\{t_{k+1}-t_{k}\}\leq\frac{1}{2} . It holds in this case that
O ⁡ ( 1 ) \displaystyle O(1)
= ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 ( φ ⁡ ( T − t ) − φ ⁡ ( T − t k ) ) ​ 𝑑 t \displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\left(\varphi(T-t)-\varphi(T-t_{k})\right)\mathrm{d}t
≳ ∑ k = M N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 ( φ ⁡ ( T − t ) − φ ⁡ ( T − t k ) ) ​ 𝑑 t \displaystyle\gtrsim\sum_{k=M}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\left(\varphi(T-t)-\varphi(T-t_{k})\right)\mathrm{d}t
≳ ∑ k = M N − 1 ( t k + 1 − t k ) 2 ​ γ ​ d ​ log ⁡ ( S ) . \displaystyle\gtrsim\sum_{k=M}^{N-1}(t_{k+1}-t_{k})^{2}\gamma d\log(S).
(82)
By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, it is direct to show that
∑ k = M N − 1 ( t k + 1 − t k ) 2 ≥ 1 N − M ​ ( ∑ k = M N − 1 ( t k + 1 − t k ) ) 2 = ( T − t M − δ ) 2 N − M ≳ 1 N − M , \displaystyle\sum_{k=M}^{N-1}(t_{k+1}-t_{k})^{2}\geq\frac{1}{N-M}\left(\sum_{k=M}^{N-1}(t_{k+1}-t_{k})\right)^{2}=\frac{(T-t_{M}-\delta)^{2}}{N-M}\gtrsim\frac{1}{N-M},
(83)
where in the last inequality, we use the fact that T − t M ≥ 1 2 T-t_{M}\geq\frac{1}{2} and δ ≪ 1 \delta\ll 1 .
Plugging Eqn. ( 83 ) into Eqn. ( 82 ) leads to
N ≥ N − M = Ω ⁡ ( γ ​ d ​ log ⁡ ( S ) ) = Ω ~ ​ ( d ) . \displaystyle N\geq N-M=\Omega(\gamma d\log(S))=\widetilde{\Omega}(d).
∎
C.4 Efficient sampling for high-entropy distributions
In the discussion following Theorem 2 , we pointed out that the τ \tau -leaping algorithm can attain sublinear iteration complexity in d d for the uniform noising process when the target distribution is close to the uniform distribution on 𝒳 {\mathcal{X}} .
We now state this result formally.
Theorem 4 .
Let q 0 ∈ 𝒫 ⁡ ( 𝒳 ) q_{0}\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathcal{X}}) denote the data distribution. Choose time points 0 = t 0 < t 1 < … < t N = T − δ 0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\ldots<t_{N}=T-\delta with exponential-then-constant step size schedule, i.e., t k + 1 − t k ≤ κ ​ min ⁡ ( 1 , T − t k + 1 ) t_{k+1}-t_{k}\leq\kappa\min(1,T-t_{k+1}) for k = 0 , … , N − 2 k=0,\ldots,N-2 . Suppose 0 < κ < 0.9 0<\kappa<0.9 . Then,
𝖪𝖫 ( q T − δ ∥ p output ) ≲ ε score + ( e − T + κ log ( δ − 1 ) ) ⋅ 𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ Unif ( 𝒳 ) ) . \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(q_{T-\delta}\,\|\,p_{\mathrm{output}})\lesssim\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+\left(e^{-T}+\kappa\log(\delta^{-1})\right)\cdot\mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\,\|\,\mathrm{Unif}({\mathcal{X}})).
Theorem 4 reveals that, with an exponential-then-constant schedule and early stopping time δ \delta , the error upper bound depends only on the initial KL divergence 𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ Unif ( 𝒳 ) ) \mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\,\|\,\mathrm{Unif}({\mathcal{X}})) , which can potentially be small if q 0 q_{0} is close to the forward limit distribution Unif ⁡ ( 𝒳 ) \mathrm{Unif}({\mathcal{X}}) .
To be more concrete, we can choose T = log ( 𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ Unif ( 𝒳 ) ) / ε ) T=\log(\mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\,\|\,\mathrm{Unif}({\mathcal{X}}))/\varepsilon) , δ − 1 = poly ⁡ ( d ) \delta^{-1}=\mathrm{poly}(d) and κ = e − T / log ⁡ ( d ) \kappa=e^{-T}/\log(d) to achieve
𝖪𝖫 ( q T − δ ∥ p output ) ≲ ε score + ε , \mathsf{KL}(q_{T-\delta}\,\|\,p_{\mathrm{output}})\lesssim\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+\varepsilon,
with iteration complexity
N = Θ ~ ​ ( 𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ Unif ( 𝒳 ) ) ε ) . \displaystyle N=\widetilde{\Theta}\left(\frac{\mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\,\|\,\mathrm{Unif}({\mathcal{X}}))}{\varepsilon}\right).
In particular, this bound is sublinear in d d when 𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ Unif ( 𝒳 ) ) = o ( d ) \mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\,\|\,\mathrm{Unif}({\mathcal{X}}))=o(d) .
Proof of Theorem 4 . The proof proceeds along the same lines as the proof of Theorem 1 .
Write p 0 = Unif ⁡ ( 𝒳 ) p_{0}=\mathrm{Unif}({\mathcal{X}}) as the initial distribution of the sampling process. Following the proof of Eqn. ( 75 ), we bound
𝖪𝖫 ( q T − δ ∥ p T − δ ) \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(q_{T-\delta}\,\|\,p_{T-\delta})
≤ ε score + e − T 𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ p 0 ) + ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 ( φ ( T − t ) − φ ( T − t k ) ) d t , \displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+e^{-T}\mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\,\|\,p_{0})+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\big(\varphi(T-t)-\varphi(T-t_{k})\big)\,\mathrm{d}t,
(84)
where as shown in Lemma 11 , φ ( t ) = − ∂ t 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ∥ p 0 ) ≥ 0 \varphi(t)=-\partial_{t}\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}\,\|\,p_{0})\geq 0 .
By Lemma 12 , φ ⁡ ( t ) \varphi(t) is a non-increasing function of t ∈ ( 0 , T ] t\in(0,T] , which leads to
φ ( t ) ≤ 1 t ∫ 0 t ∂ s 𝖪𝖫 ( q s ∥ p 0 ) d s = 𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ p 0 ) t . \displaystyle\varphi(t)\leq\frac{1}{t}\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{s}\mathsf{KL}(q_{s}\,\|\,p_{0})\mathrm{d}s=\frac{\mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\,\|\,p_{0})}{t}.
(85)
Without loss of generality, Choose M M such that 1 ≤ M ≤ N − 1 1\leq M\leq N-1 such that T − t M = 1 T-t_{M}=1 . For 1 ≤ k < M 1\leq k<M , t k + 1 − t k = t k − t k − 1 = κ t_{k+1}-t_{k}=t_{k}-t_{k-1}=\kappa .
With Eqn. ( 85 ), it can be seen that
∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 ( φ ⁡ ( T − t ) − φ ⁡ ( T − t k ) ) ​ 𝑑 t \displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\big(\varphi(T-t)-\varphi(T-t_{k})\big)\,\mathrm{d}t
≤ ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ( t k + 1 − t k ) ​ ( φ ⁡ ( T − t k + 1 ) − φ ⁡ ( T − t k ) ) ​ d ​ t \displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}({t_{k+1}}-t_{k})\big(\varphi(T-t_{k+1})-\varphi(T-t_{k})\big)\,\mathrm{d}t
= ( t N − t N − 1 ) ​ φ ​ ( T − t N ) + ∑ k = 1 N − 1 ( ( t k − t k − 1 ) + ( t k − t k + 1 ) ) ​ φ ​ ( T − t k ) − ( t 1 − t 0 ) ​ φ ​ ( T ) \displaystyle=(t_{N}-t_{N-1})\varphi(T-t_{N})+\sum_{k=1}^{N-1}\big((t_{k}-t_{k-1})+(t_{k}-t_{k+1})\big)\varphi(T-t_{k})-(t_{1}-t_{0})\varphi(T)
≤ ( a ) ​ κ ​ δ 1 − κ ⋅ 𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ p 0 ) δ + ∑ k = M N − 1 κ 2 1 − κ ​ ( T − t k ) ⋅ 𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ p 0 ) T − t k \displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{\leq}\frac{\kappa\delta}{1-\kappa}\cdot\frac{\mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\,\|\,p_{0})}{\delta}+\sum_{k=M}^{N-1}\frac{\kappa^{2}}{1-\kappa}(T-t_{k})\cdot\frac{\mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\,\|\,p_{0})}{T-t_{k}}
≲ ( N − M ) κ 2 𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ p 0 ) = log ( 1 − κ ) ( δ ) κ 2 𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ p 0 ) ≤ ( b ) κ log ( δ − 1 ) 𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ p 0 ) , \displaystyle\lesssim(N-M)\kappa^{2}\mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\,\|\,p_{0})=\log_{(1-\kappa)}(\delta)\kappa^{2}\mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\,\|\,p_{0})\overset{\mathrm{(b)}}{\leq}\kappa\log(\delta^{-1})\mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\,\|\,p_{0}),
where we apply Eqn. ( 85 ) in (a) and log ⁡ ( 1 − κ ) ≤ − κ \log(1-\kappa)\leq-\kappa in (b).
Putting the above bound and Eqn. ( 84 ) together proves the desired result. ∎
Appendix D Proofs of results in Section 3.2
D.1 Proof of Theorem 3
For t ∈ { t 0 , … , t N } t\in\{t_{0},\ldots,t_{N}\} , let p t p_{t} denote the marginal distribution of x t k x_{t_{k}} in Algorithm 1 .
Using the data-processing inequality 𝖪𝖫 ( q ← T ∥ p T ) ≤ 𝖪𝖫 ( q ← t 0 , … , t N ∥ p t 0 , … , t N ) \mathsf{KL}(\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{T}\|p_{T})\leq\mathsf{KL}(\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t_{0},\ldots,t_{N}}\|p_{t_{0},\ldots,t_{N}}) and Lemma 4 , we decompose the KL divergence between the target distribution q 0 ≡ q ← T q_{0}\equiv\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{T} and the output distribution p T p_{T} as follows:
𝖪𝖫 ( q ← T ∥ p T ) ≤ 𝖪𝖫 ( q ← 0 ∥ p 0 ) + ∑ k = 0 N − 1 𝔼 x t k ∼ q ← t k [ 𝖪𝖫 ( q ← t k + 1 | t k ( ⋅ | x t k ) ∥ p t k + 1 | t k ( ⋅ | x t k ) ) ] . \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{T}\|p_{T})\leq\mathsf{KL}(\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{0}\|p_{0})+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t_{k}}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t_{k}}}\left[\mathsf{KL}\left(\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t_{k+1}|t_{k}}(\cdot|x_{t_{k}})\|p_{t_{k+1}|t_{k}}(\cdot|x_{t_{k}})\right)\right].
(86)
The first term, initialization error , was bounded in Conforti et al. (2025) ; Liang et al. (2025b) as follows:
𝖪𝖫 ( q ← 0 ∥ p 0 ) ≤ e − T d ( 1 + log S + T ) ≲ e − T d log S . \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{0}\|p_{0})\leq e^{-T}d(1+\log S+T)\lesssim e^{-T}d\log S.
(87)
Next, we move on to control the second term.
The following lemma states that for each k k , conditioned on x t k x_{t_{k}} , one can consider a CTMC on the interval [ t k , t k + 1 ] [t_{k},t_{k+1}] , with marginals p t k + 1 | t k ( ⋅ ∣ x t k ) p_{t_{k+1}\mid t_{k}}(\cdot\mid x_{t_{k}}) at time t k + 1 t_{k+1} . The proof is given in Section F.3 .
Lemma 13 .
Fix k = 0 , … , N − 1 k=0,\ldots,N-1 . Let x t k x_{t_{k}} and x t k + 1 x_{t_{k+1}} be as in Algorithm 1 .
Let ( y t ) t ∈ [ t k , t k + 1 ] (y_{t})_{t\in[t_{k},t_{k+1}]} be a CTMC with y t k = x t k y_{t_{k}}=x_{t_{k}} and the following rate matrix:
Q ^ t ​ ( a , b ) ≔ { s ^ T − t k ( y t k ⊙ i b i , y t k ) e T − t k − 1 e T − t − 1 𝕀 { a i = MASK } , if d_H ( a , b ) = 1 , a i ≠ b i , and y t k i = MASK , − ∑ c ≠ a Q ^ t ( a , c ) , if ​ a = b , 0 , otherwise. \displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{t}(a,b)\coloneqq\begin{cases}\widehat{s}_{T-t_{k}}(y_{t_{k}}\odot_{i}b^{i},y_{t_{k}})\frac{e^{T-t_{k}}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}{\mathbb{I}}\{a^{i}=\mathrm{MASK}\},&\kern 5.0pt\text{if }\ham(a,b)=1,a^{i}\neq b^{i},\text{ and }y_{t_{k}}^{i}=\mathrm{MASK},\\
-\sum_{c\neq a}\widehat{Q}_{t}(a,c),&\kern 5.0pt\text{if }a=b,\\
0,&\kern 5.0pt\text{otherwise.}\\
\end{cases}
(88)
Then, x t k + 1 x_{t_{k+1}} has the same distribution as y t k + 1 y_{t_{k+1}} .
Armed with this result, we rewrite the right hand side of Equation 86 with marginals p t | t k ( ⋅ ∣ x t k ) p_{t\mid t_{k}}(\cdot\mid x_{t_{k}}) of this CTMC:
𝖪𝖫 ( q ← T ∥ p T ) \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{T}\|p_{T})
≲ e − T d log S + ∑ k = 0 N − 1 𝔼 x t k ∼ q ← t k [ 𝖪𝖫 ( q ← t k + 1 | t k ( ⋅ | x t k ) ∥ p t k + 1 | t k ( ⋅ | x t k ) ) ] \displaystyle\lesssim e^{-T}d\log S+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t_{k}}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t_{k}}}\left[\mathsf{KL}\left(\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t_{k+1}|t_{k}}(\cdot|x_{t_{k}})\|p_{t_{k+1}|t_{k}}(\cdot|x_{t_{k}})\right)\right]
(89)
= e − T d log S + ∑ k = 0 N − 1 𝔼 x t k ∼ q ← t k [ ∫ t k t k + 1 ∂ ∂ t 𝖪𝖫 ( q ← t | t k ( ⋅ ∣ x t k ) ∥ p t | t k ( ⋅ ∣ x t k ) ) d t ] . \displaystyle=e^{-T}d\log S+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t_{k}}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t_{k}}}\left[\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathsf{KL}\left(\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid t_{k}}(\cdot\mid x_{t_{k}})\|p_{t\mid t_{k}}(\cdot\mid x_{t_{k}})\right)\mathrm{d}t\right].
(90)
To further control the second term above, we apply Lemma 5 with rate matrices specified in Lemma 13 . We can write
𝖪𝖫 ( q ← T ∥ p T ) \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{T}\|p_{T})
≲ e − T ​ d ​ log ⁡ S + ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 𝔼 x t k , x t ∼ q ← t k , t ​ ∑ y ≠ x t [ Q ^ t ​ ( x t , y ) − Q ← t ​ ( x t , y ) + Q ← t ​ ( x t , y ) ​ log ⁡ ( Q ← t ​ ( x t , y ) Q ^ t ​ ( x t , y ) ) ] ​ 𝑑 t . \displaystyle\lesssim e^{-T}d\log S+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t_{k}},x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{{t_{k},t}}}\sum_{y\neq x_{t}}\left[\widehat{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)-\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)+\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)\log\left(\frac{\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)}{\widehat{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)}\right)\right]\mathrm{d}t.
(91)
Fix k ∈ { 0 , … , N − 1 } k\in\{0,\ldots,N-1\} and t ∈ [ t k , t k + 1 ) t\in[t_{k},t_{k+1}) . Let ℓ ≔ t k \ell\coloneqq t_{k} .
Invoking Eqn. ( 88 ) further leads to
∑ y ≠ x t [ Q ^ t ​ ( x t , y ) − Q ← t ​ ( x t , y ) + Q ← t ​ ( x t , y ) ​ log ⁡ ( Q ← t ​ ( x t , y ) Q ^ t ​ ( x t , y ) ) ] \displaystyle\sum_{y\neq x_{t}}\left[\widehat{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)-\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)+\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)\log\left(\frac{\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)}{\widehat{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)}\right)\right]
= ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] [ Q ^ t ​ ( x t , x t ⊙ i c ) − Q ← t ​ ( x t , x t ⊙ i c ) + Q ← t ​ ( x t , x t ⊙ i c ) ​ log ⁡ ( Q ← t ​ ( x t , x t ⊙ i c ) Q ^ t ​ ( x t , x t ⊙ i c ) ) ] \displaystyle\kern 5.0pt=\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}\left[\widehat{Q}_{t}(x_{t},x_{t}\odot_{i}c)-\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},x_{t}\odot_{i}c)+\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},x_{t}\odot_{i}c)\log\left(\frac{\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},x_{t}\odot_{i}c)}{\widehat{Q}_{t}(x_{t},x_{t}\odot_{i}c)}\right)\right]
= ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] [ e T − ℓ − 1 e T − t − 1 s ^ T − ℓ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) − s T − t ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) \displaystyle\kern 5.0pt=\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}\Bigg[\frac{e^{T-\ell}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})
+ s T − t ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) log ( s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) e T − ℓ − 1 e T − t − 1 ​ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) ) ] \displaystyle\hskip 150.00023pt+s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})\log\left(\frac{s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})}{\frac{e^{T-\ell}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})}\right)\Bigg]
= ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ​ D ​ ( e T − ℓ − 1 e T − t − 1 ​ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ) . \displaystyle\kern 5.0pt=\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})D\left(\frac{e^{T-\ell}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})\right).
(92)
To proceed, we make the observation that the Bregman divergence satisfies the following law of cosines:
D ⁡ ( α , γ ) = D ⁡ ( α , β ) + D ⁡ ( β , γ ) + ( α − β ) ​ β − γ β ​ γ . \displaystyle D(\alpha,\gamma)=D(\alpha,\beta)+D(\beta,\gamma)+(\alpha-\beta)\frac{\beta-\gamma}{\beta\gamma}.
We apply this decomposition to each term of Equation 92 with
α = e T − ℓ − 1 e T − t − 1 s ^ T − ℓ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) , β = e T − ℓ − 1 e T − t − 1 s T − ℓ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) , and γ = s T − t ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) . \displaystyle\alpha=\frac{e^{T-\ell}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}),\qquad\beta=\frac{e^{T-\ell}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}),\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma=s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t}).
In the following, we slightly abuse the notation and write x t ≔ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) x_{t}\coloneqq(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t}) and x ℓ ≔ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) x_{\ell}\coloneqq(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}) whenever i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) i\in m(x_{t}) and c ∈ [ S ] c\in[S] are fixed.
For fixed i , c i,c , each term in Eqn. ( 92 ) can be decomposed as
s T − t ​ ( x t ) ​ D ​ ( e T − ℓ − 1 e T − t − 1 ​ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) \displaystyle s_{T-t}(x_{t})D\left(\frac{e^{T-\ell}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{t})\right)
= s T − t ​ ( x t ) ​ D ​ ( s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) , s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) ) \displaystyle=s_{T-t}(x_{t})D(\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}),s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}))
+ s T − t ​ ( x t ) ​ D ​ ( e T − ℓ − 1 e T − t − 1 ​ s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) \displaystyle\quad+s_{T-t}(x_{t})D\left(\frac{e^{T-\ell}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{t})\right)
+ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) − s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) ​ ( e T − ℓ − 1 e T − t − 1 ​ s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) − s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) . \displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})-s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}{s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}\left(\frac{e^{T-\ell}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{t})\right).
Note that we simplified the first term as D ⁡ ( α ​ x , α ​ y ) = D ⁡ ( x , y ) D(\alpha x,\alpha y)=D(x,y) .
Observing that e T − ℓ − 1 e T − t − 1 ​ s T − ℓ ≡ s T − t \frac{e^{T-\ell}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}s_{T-\ell}\equiv s_{T-t} by Equation 60 , this can be rearranged as follows:
s T − t ​ ( x t ) ​ D ​ ( e T − ℓ − 1 e T − t − 1 ​ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) \displaystyle s_{T-t}(x_{t})D\left(\frac{e^{T-\ell}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{t})\right)
= e T − ℓ − 1 e T − t − 1 ​ s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) ​ D ​ ( s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) , s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) ) ⏟ ≕ T 1 \displaystyle=\underbrace{\frac{e^{T-\ell}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})D(\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}),s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}))}_{\eqqcolon\,T_{1}}
+ ( s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ) − s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) ⋅ log ⁡ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) ⏟ ≕ T 2 \displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\underbrace{\big(s_{T-t}(x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{t})\big)\cdot\log\frac{\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}{s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}}_{\eqqcolon\,T_{2}}
+ s T − t ​ ( x t ) ​ D ​ ( s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) ⏟ ≕ T 3 . \displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+\underbrace{s_{T-t}(x_{t})D\left(s_{T-t}(x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{t})\right)}_{\eqqcolon\,T_{3}}.
Taking the above collectively with Eqns. ( 92 ) and ( 91 ) leads to
𝖪𝖫 ( q ← T ∥ p T ) \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{T}\|p_{T})
≲ e − T ​ d ​ log ⁡ S + ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 𝔼 x t k , x t ∼ q ← t k , t ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] ( T 1 + T 2 + T 3 ) . \displaystyle\lesssim e^{-T}d\log S+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t_{k}},x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{{t_{k},t}}}\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}(T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}).
Now, it suffices to control each term on the right, respectively.
•
After taking a summation over i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) i\in m(x_{t}) and c ∈ [ S ] c\in[S] we connect the first term, T 1 T_{1} , to the score entropy loss.
To see that, direct calculations show
𝔼 x t , x ℓ ∼ q ← t , ℓ ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] e T − ℓ − 1 e T − t − 1 ​ s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) ​ D ​ ( s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) , s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) ) \displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t},x_{\ell}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t,\ell}}\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}\frac{e^{T-\ell}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})D(\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}),s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}))
= 𝔼 x ℓ ∼ q ← ℓ ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x ℓ ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] e t − ℓ ​ s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) ​ D ​ ( s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) , s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) ) \displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{x_{\ell}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{\ell}}\sum_{i\in m(x_{\ell})}\sum_{c\in[S]}e^{t-\ell}s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})D(\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}),s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}))
= e t − ℓ ​ ℒ SE ​ ( T − ℓ , s ^ T − ℓ , s T − ℓ ) , \displaystyle=e^{t-\ell}{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{SE}}(T-\ell,\widehat{s}_{T-\ell},s_{T-\ell}),
where we used in the second line that Pr ⁡ ( x t i = MASK ∣ x ℓ i = MASK ) = 1 − e − ( T − t ) 1 − e − ( T − ℓ ) \Pr(x_{t}^{i}=\mathrm{MASK}\mid x_{\ell}^{i}=\mathrm{MASK})=\frac{1-e^{-(T-t)}}{1-e^{-(T-\ell)}} .
Therefore, recalling that ℓ ≔ t k \ell\coloneqq t_{k} ,
∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 e t − t k ​ ℒ SE ​ ( T − t k , s ^ T − t k , s T − t k ) ​ 𝑑 t \displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}e^{t-t_{k}}{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{SE}}(T-t_{k},\widehat{s}_{T-t_{k}},s_{T-t_{k}})\mathrm{d}t
= ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ( e t k + 1 − t k − 1 ) ​ ℒ SE ​ ( T − t k , s ^ T − t k , s T − t k ) ≲ ε score , \displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}(e^{t_{k+1}-t_{k}}-1){\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{SE}}(T-t_{k},\widehat{s}_{T-t_{k}},s_{T-t_{k}})\lesssim\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}},
(93)
where we used Δ = O ⁡ ( 1 ) \Delta=O(1) and Assumption 1 in the last inequality.
•
To control the second term, T 2 T_{2} , the next lemma describes a martingale property of the score function. The proof is given in Section E.7 .
Lemma 14 .
Consider the masking noising process and let 0 ≤ ℓ < t < T 0\leq\ell<t<T . Then, for any c ∈ 𝒱 c\in{\mathcal{V}} and i ∈ m ⁡ ( x ℓ ) i\in m(x_{\ell}) ,
𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ ( s T − t ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) − s T − t ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ) 𝕀 { i ∈ m ( x t ) } ] = 0 . \displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}\left[\left(s_{T-t}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})\right){\mathbb{I}}\{i\in m(x_{t})\}\right]=0.
In view of Lemma 14 , we conclude that the second term, T 2 T_{2} , contributes zero after conditioning on x t k x_{t_{k}} :
∑ i ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ∈ [ S ] 𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | t k ( ⋅ ∣ x t k ) 𝕀 { i ∈ m ( x t ) } ( s T − t ( x t k ⊙ i c , x t k ) − s T − t ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ) = 0 . \displaystyle\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{c\in[S]}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid t_{k}}{(\cdot\mid x_{t_{k}})}}{\mathbb{I}}\{i\in m(x_{t})\}(s_{T-t}(x_{t_{k}}\odot_{i}c,x_{t_{k}})-s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t}))=0.
•
Lastly, we move on to control the last term, T 3 T_{3} . Towards this goal, we introduce the following lemma, whose proof is provided in Section F.4 .
Lemma 15 .
Let 0 ≤ ℓ < t ≤ T 0\leq\ell<t\leq T . Then, for ℐ ⁡ ( t ) {\mathcal{I}}(t) defined in Equation 16 ,
𝔼 x ℓ , x t ∼ q ← ℓ , t ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ​ D ​ ( s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ) \displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{\ell},x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{\ell,t}}\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})D\left(s_{T-t}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})\right)
= ∫ ℓ t e t − v ​ ℐ ​ ( T − v ) ​ 𝑑 v . \displaystyle\kern 5.0pt=\int_{\ell}^{t}e^{t-v}{\mathcal{I}}(T-v)\mathrm{d}v.
(94)
After the summation over i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) i\in m(x_{t}) and c ∈ [ S ] c\in[S] , we express the contributions of the term T 3 T_{3} using Lemma 15 as follows:
∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 𝔼 x t k , x t ∼ q ← t k , t ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ​ D ​ ( s T − t ​ ( x t k ⊙ i c , x t k ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ) ​ 𝑑 t \displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t_{k}},x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t_{k},t}}\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})D\left(s_{T-t}(x_{t_{k}}\odot_{i}c,x_{t_{k}}),s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})\right)\mathrm{d}t
= ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 ∫ t k t e t − v ​ ℐ ​ ( T − v ) ​ 𝑑 v ​ 𝑑 t ≤ ∑ k = 0 N − 1 h k ​ ∫ T − t k + 1 T − t k ℐ ⁡ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t , \displaystyle\quad=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\int_{t_{k}}^{t}e^{t-v}{\mathcal{I}}(T-v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}t\leq\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k}\int_{T-t_{k+1}}^{T-t_{k}}{\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t,
(95)
where we used Δ = O ⁡ ( 1 ) \Delta=O(1) and non-negativity of conditional mutual information in the last inequality.
Collecting Eqns. ( 87 ), ( 93 ), and ( 95 ) proves
𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ p T ) ≲ ε score + e − T d log S + ∑ k = 0 N − 1 h k ∫ T − t k + 1 T − t k ℐ ( t ) d t . \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\,\|\,p_{T})\lesssim\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+e^{-T}d\log S+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k}\int_{T-t_{k+1}}^{T-t_{k}}{\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t.
∎
D.2 Proof of Corollary 2
We upper bound the last term of Equation 17 under uniform and exponential-then-constant step size schedules.
First, under the constant step size schedule, the quantity of interest satisfies
∑ k = 0 N − 1 h k ​ ∫ T − t k + 1 T − t k ℐ ⁡ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t = T N ​ ∫ 0 T ℐ ⁡ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t ≤ T N ​ ∫ 0 ∞ ℐ ⁡ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t = T N ​ ℬ ​ ( q 0 ) , \displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k}\int_{T-t_{k+1}}^{T-t_{k}}{\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t=\frac{T}{N}\int_{0}^{T}{\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t\leq\frac{T}{N}\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t=\frac{T}{N}{\mathcal{B}}(q_{0}),
where the last inequality follows from Lemma 16 .
Therefore, as long as
N ≥ T ​ ℬ ​ ( q 0 ) ε = O ~ ​ ( ℬ ⁡ ( q 0 ) ε ) , \displaystyle N\geq\frac{T{\mathcal{B}}(q_{0})}{\varepsilon}=\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{B}}(q_{0})}{\varepsilon}\right),
Equation 17 leads to 𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ p T ) ≲ ε score + ε \mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\|p_{T})\lesssim\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+\varepsilon .
Next, under exponential-then-constant step size schedule, we bound the last term of Equation 17 as follows:
∑ k = 0 N − 1 h k ​ ∫ T − t k + 1 T − t k ℐ ⁡ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t = ε d ​ log ⁡ ( S ) ​ ∫ 0 ε / ( d ​ log ⁡ S ) ℐ ⁡ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t + ∑ k = 0 N − 2 ∫ T − t k + 1 T − t k ( t k + 1 − t k ) ​ ℐ ​ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t \displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k}\int_{T-t_{k+1}}^{T-t_{k}}{\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t=\frac{\varepsilon}{d\log(S)}\int_{0}^{\varepsilon/(d\log S)}{\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t+\sum_{k=0}^{N-2}\int_{T-t_{k+1}}^{T-t_{k}}(t_{k+1}-t_{k}){\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t
≤ ε 2 log ⁡ ( S ) + κ ​ ∑ k = 0 N − 2 ∫ T − t k + 1 T − t k min ⁡ ( 1 , T − t k + 1 ) ​ ℐ ​ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t ≤ ε + κ ​ ∫ 0 T min ⁡ ( 1 , t ) ​ ℐ ​ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t ≤ ε + κ ​ 𝒟 ​ ( q 0 ) . \displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{\log(S)}+\kappa\sum_{k=0}^{N-2}\int_{T-t_{k+1}}^{T-t_{k}}\min(1,T-t_{k+1}){\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t\leq\varepsilon+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\min(1,t){\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t\leq\varepsilon+\kappa{\mathcal{D}}(q_{0}).
For N > 0 N>0 , such step size schedule is possible with
κ = O ⁡ ( T + log ⁡ ( ε − 1 ​ d ​ log ⁡ ( S ) ) N ) \kappa=O\left(\frac{T+\log(\varepsilon^{-1}d\log(S))}{N}\right) .
Thus, choosing
N ≥ ( T + log ⁡ ( ε − 1 ​ d ​ log ⁡ ( S ) ) ) ​ 𝒟 ​ ( q 0 ) ε = O ~ ​ ( 𝒟 ⁡ ( q 0 ) ε ) \displaystyle N\geq\frac{(T+\log(\varepsilon^{-1}d\log(S))){\mathcal{D}}(q_{0})}{\varepsilon}=\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{D}}(q_{0})}{\varepsilon}\right)
gives 𝖪𝖫 ( q 0 ∥ p T ) ≲ ε score + ε \mathsf{KL}(q_{0}\,\|\,p_{T})\lesssim\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+\varepsilon . ∎
D.3 τ \tau -leaping for masking discrete diffusion
In this section, we prove the analogue of Theorem 3 for the truncated τ \tau -leaping algorithm.
Note that since applying multiple jumps on a single coordinate is ill-defined in masking noising process (where should we transition if the τ \tau -leaping algorithm requires two transitions MASK → 1 \mathrm{MASK}\to 1 at some coordinate?), we analyse the truncated version ( Equation 9 ) instead of the classical τ \tau -leaping algorithm.
Theorem 5 .
Let q data = q 0 q_{\mathrm{data}}=q_{0} be the target distribution on [ S ] d [S]^{d} . Let 0 < δ < T 0<\delta<T and 0 = t 0 < t 1 < … < t N = T − δ 0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\ldots<t_{N}=T-\delta , such that h k ≔ t k − t k − 1 ≤ κ ​ min ⁡ ( 1 , T − t k ) h_{k}\coloneqq t_{k}-t_{k-1}\leq\kappa\min(1,T-t_{k}) for k ∈ [ N ] k\in[N] , and κ = O ⁡ ( 1 ) \kappa=O(1) .
Let
p 0 ≔ ( ( 1 − e − T ) ​ δ MASK + e − T S ​ ∑ k = 1 S δ k ) ⊗ d . \displaystyle p_{0}\coloneqq\left(\big(1-e^{-T}\big)\delta_{\mathrm{MASK}}+\frac{e^{-T}}{S}\sum_{k=1}^{S}\delta_{k}\right)^{\otimes d}.
Under Assumption 1 , truncated τ \tau -leaping Equation 9 initialized at p 0 p_{0}
produces a sample from p output ≔ p T − δ p_{\mathrm{output}}\coloneqq p_{T-\delta} , such that
𝖪𝖫 ( q δ ∥ p output ) ≲ ε score + e − T d log ( S ) + ∑ k = 0 N − 1 h k + 1 ∫ T − t k + 1 T − t k ℐ ( t ) d t + κ 3 N d + κ C , \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(q_{\delta}\,\|\,p_{\mathrm{output}})\lesssim\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+e^{-T}d\log(S)+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k+1}\int_{T-t_{k+1}}^{T-t_{k}}{\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t+\kappa^{3}Nd+\kappa C,
(96)
where
C ≔ ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ( t k + 1 − t k ) ​ 𝔼 x t k ∼ q ← t k ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t k ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s T − t k ​ ( x t k ⊙ i c , x t k ) ​ | log ⁡ s ^ T − t k ​ ( x t k ⊙ i c , x t k ) s T − t k ​ ( x t k ⊙ i c , x t k ) | . \displaystyle C\coloneqq\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}(t_{k+1}-t_{k}){\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t_{k}}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t_{k}}}\sum_{i\in m(x_{t_{k}})}\sum_{c\in[S]}s_{T-{t_{k}}}(x_{t_{k}}\odot_{i}c,x_{t_{k}})\left\lvert\log\frac{\widehat{s}_{T-{t_{k}}}(x_{t_{k}}\odot_{i}c,x_{t_{k}})}{s_{T-{t_{k}}}(x_{t_{k}}\odot_{i}c,x_{t_{k}})}\right\rvert.
Consequently, with exponential-then-constant step size schedule where κ = O ⁡ ( T + log ⁡ ( δ − 1 ) N ) \kappa=O\left(\frac{T+\log(\delta^{-1})}{N}\right) , for any ε > 0 \varepsilon>0 , for T = O ⁡ ( log ⁡ ( ε − 1 ​ d ​ log ⁡ S ) ) T=O(\log(\varepsilon^{-1}d\log S)) and
N = O ~ ​ ( 𝒟 ⁡ ( q data ) + C ε + d ε ) , \displaystyle N=\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{D}}(q_{\mathrm{data}})+C}{\varepsilon}+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\varepsilon}}\right),
(97)
it satisfies
𝖪𝖫 ( q δ ∥ p output ) ≲ ε score + ε . \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(q_{\delta}\|p_{\mathrm{output}})\lesssim\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+\varepsilon.
Note that the guarantee in Equation 96 closely parallels Equation 17 in Theorem 3 for Algorithm 1 . In particular, two additional terms arise in the analysis of the truncated τ \tau -leaping algorithm.
We expect the constant C C to be small and remark that it also arises in the analysis of Conforti et al. (2025) , as C 2 M C_{2}^{M} in Theorem 3.2.1, in the form of the maximum rather than the average. Under the assumption of (one-sided) boundedness s ^ T − t k ≥ M − 1 \widehat{s}_{T-t_{k}}\geq M^{-1} , the constant C C can be upper bounded via the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, as the next corollary shows.
Corollary 3 .
Consider the setting of Theorem 5 . If, additionally, there exists M > 0 M>0 such that for all k ∈ { 0 , … , N − 1 } k\in\{0,\ldots,N-1\} , x ∈ ( [ S ] ∪ { MASK } ) d x\in([S]\cup\{\mathrm{MASK}\})^{d} , i ∈ m ⁡ ( x ) i\in m(x) , and c ∈ [ S ] c\in[S] it holds log ⁡ s ^ T − t k ​ ( x ⊙ i c , x ) ≥ − log ⁡ M \log\widehat{s}_{T-t_{k}}(x\odot_{i}c,x)\geq-\log M , it is sufficient for
N = O ~ ​ ( 𝒟 ⁡ ( q data ) + ε score ​ d ​ log ⁡ M ε + d ε ) \displaystyle N=\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{D}}(q_{\mathrm{data}})+\sqrt{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}d\log M}}{\varepsilon}+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\varepsilon}}\right)
to ensure
𝖪𝖫 ( q δ ∥ p T − δ ) ≲ ε score + ε . \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(q_{\delta}\|p_{T-\delta})\lesssim\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+\varepsilon.

(98)
where in ( a ) (a) we used t k + 1 − t k ≤ κ t_{k+1}-t_{k}\leq\kappa together with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
Combining the bound of Equation 98 with Equation 97 and κ = O ~ ​ ( 1 / N ) \kappa=\widetilde{O}(1/N) completes the proof.
∎
We emphasize that, in contrast to Theorem 3 , in Theorem 5 we require early stopping for some δ > 0 \delta>0 , which in turn leads to the exponential-then-constant step size schedule.
We now elaborate on the difference between Theorem 5 and Theorem 3 , and provide some intuition for the appearance of the two additional terms in Eqn. ( 96 ).
Remark 3 .
To obtain an accurate sampler, it is natural to require that Q ^ t ≈ Q ← t \widehat{Q}_{t}\approx\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t} uniformly for all t ∈ [ 0 , T ] t\in[0,T] . The main challenge is that we only have access to score estimates at discrete time points.
In the truncated τ \tau -leaping algorithm analyzed in this section, this results in approximating
s ^ T − t ≔ s ^ T − t k ≈ s T − t . \displaystyle\widehat{s}_{T-t}\coloneqq\widehat{s}_{T-t_{k}}\approx s_{T-t}.
Informally, we establish this by showing
s ^ T − t k ≈ s T − t k ≈ s T − t , \displaystyle\widehat{s}_{T-t_{k}}\approx s_{T-t_{k}}\approx s_{T-t},
(99)
where the first approximation is ensured by Assumption 1 and the second results from the properties of the score function for the masking noising process, requiring the step size t k + 1 − t k t_{k+1}-t_{k} to be small.
In contrast, for Algorithm 1 considered in Theorem 3 , the condition Q ^ t ≈ Q ← t \widehat{Q}_{t}\approx\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t} translates to
s ^ T − t ≔ e T − t k − 1 e T − t − 1 ​ s ^ T − t k ≈ s T − t . \displaystyle\widehat{s}_{T-t}\coloneqq\frac{e^{T-t_{k}}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}\widehat{s}_{T-t_{k}}\approx s_{T-t}.
In view of Proposition 6 , the above condition is equivalent to
s ^ T − t ≔ e T − t k − 1 e T − t − 1 ​ s ^ T − t k ≈ e T − t k − 1 e T − t − 1 ​ s T − t k = s T − t , \displaystyle\widehat{s}_{T-t}\coloneqq\frac{e^{T-t_{k}}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}\widehat{s}_{T-t_{k}}\approx\frac{e^{T-t_{k}}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}s_{T-t_{k}}=s_{T-t},
which is guaranteed by Assumption 1 .
Notably, this simple rescaling eliminates the need for the second approximation step that is required in the truncated τ \tau -leaping analysis. This distinction explains why Theorem 5 contains two additional error terms and necessitates early stopping, in contrast to the cleaner guarantee obtained in Theorem 3 .
Proof of Theorem 5 . The proof follows the proof of Theorem 3 closely with several additional steps. We begin with Equation 91
𝖪𝖫 ( q ← T ∥ p T ) \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{T}\|p_{T})
≲ e − T ​ d ​ log ⁡ S + ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 𝔼 x t k , x t ∼ q ← t k , t ​ ∑ y ≠ x t [ Q ^ t ​ ( x t , y ) − Q ← t ​ ( x t , y ) + Q ← t ​ ( x t , y ) ​ log ⁡ ( Q ← t ​ ( x t , y ) Q ^ t ​ ( x t , y ) ) ] ​ 𝑑 t . \displaystyle\lesssim e^{-T}d\log S+\!\!\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\!\!{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t_{k}},x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{{t_{k},t}}}\sum_{y\neq x_{t}}\left[\widehat{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)-\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)+\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)\log\left(\frac{\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)}{\widehat{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)}\right)\right]\mathrm{d}t.
(100)
Next, since for this sampler, the rate matrices Q ^ t \widehat{Q}_{t} are given by the following:
Q ^ t ​ ( x , y ) ≔ { s ^ T − t k ( x t k ⊙ i y i , x t k ) 𝕀 { x i = MASK } , if d_H ( x , y ) = 1 , x i ≠ y i , and x t k i = MASK , − ∑ z ≠ x Q ^ t ( x , z ) , if ​ y = x , 0 , otherwise, \displaystyle\widehat{Q}_{t}(x,y)\coloneqq\begin{cases}\widehat{s}_{T-t_{k}}(x_{t_{k}}\odot_{i}y^{i},x_{t_{k}}){\mathbb{I}}\{x^{i}=\mathrm{MASK}\},&\quad\text{if }\ham(x,y)=1,x^{i}\neq y^{i},\text{ and }x_{t_{k}}^{i}=\mathrm{MASK},\\
-\sum_{z\neq x}\widehat{Q}_{t}(x,z),&\quad\text{if }y=x,\\
0,&\quad\text{otherwise,}\\
\end{cases}
we can therefore bound
∑ y ≠ x t [ Q ^ t ​ ( x t , y ) − Q ← t ​ ( x t , y ) + Q ← t ​ ( x t , y ) ​ log ⁡ ( Q ← t ​ ( x t , y ) Q ^ t ​ ( x t , y ) ) ] \displaystyle\sum_{y\neq x_{t}}\left[\widehat{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)-\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)+\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)\log\left(\frac{\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)}{\widehat{Q}_{t}(x_{t},y)}\right)\right]
= ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] [ Q ^ t ​ ( x t , x t ⊙ i c ) − Q ← t ​ ( x t , x t ⊙ i c ) + Q ← t ​ ( x t , x t ⊙ i c ) ​ log ⁡ ( Q ← t ​ ( x t , x t ⊙ i c ) Q ^ t ​ ( x t , x t ⊙ i c ) ) ] \displaystyle\kern 5.0pt=\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}\left[\widehat{Q}_{t}(x_{t},x_{t}\odot_{i}c)-\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},x_{t}\odot_{i}c)+\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},x_{t}\odot_{i}c)\log\left(\frac{\overset{\leftarrow}{Q}_{t}(x_{t},x_{t}\odot_{i}c)}{\widehat{Q}_{t}(x_{t},x_{t}\odot_{i}c)}\right)\right]
= ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] [ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) − s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) + s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ​ log ⁡ ( s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) ) ] \displaystyle\kern 5.0pt=\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}\left[\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})+s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})\log\left(\frac{s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})}{\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})}\right)\right]
= ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ​ D ​ ( s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ) . \displaystyle\kern 5.0pt=\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})D\left(\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})\right).
To proceed, we again apply the law of cosines D ⁡ ( α , γ ) = D ⁡ ( α , β ) + D ⁡ ( β , γ ) + ( α − β ) ​ β − γ β ​ γ D(\alpha,\gamma)=D(\alpha,\beta)+D(\beta,\gamma)+(\alpha-\beta)\frac{\beta-\gamma}{\beta\gamma} with
α = s ^ T − ℓ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) , β = s T − ℓ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) , and γ = s T − t ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) . \displaystyle\alpha=\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}),\qquad\beta=s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}),\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma=s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t}).
In the following, we slightly abuse the notation and write x t ≔ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) x_{t}\coloneqq(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t}) and x ℓ ≔ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) x_{\ell}\coloneqq(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}) whenever i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) i\in m(x_{t}) and c ∈ [ S ] c\in[S] are fixed.
As a result, for fixed i , c i,c , one has
s T − t ​ ( x t ) ​ D ​ ( s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) \displaystyle s_{T-t}(x_{t})D\left(\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{t})\right)
= s T − t ​ ( x t ) ​ D ​ ( s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) , s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) ) + s T − t ​ ( x t ) ​ D ​ ( s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) \displaystyle=s_{T-t}(x_{t})D(\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}),s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}))+s_{T-t}(x_{t})D\left(s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{t})\right)
+ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) − s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) ​ ( s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) − s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) . \displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})-s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}{s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}\left(s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{t})\right).
This can be rearranged as follows:
s T − t ​ ( x t ) ​ D ​ ( s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) \displaystyle s_{T-t}(x_{t})D\left(\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{t})\right)

+ s T − t ​ ( x t ) ​ D ​ ( s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) ⏟ ≕ T 2 \displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{s_{T-t}(x_{t})D\left(s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{t})\right)}_{\eqqcolon\,T_{2}}
+ ( s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) − s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) ​ log ⁡ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) ⏟ ≕ T 3 . \displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\left(s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{t})\right)\log\frac{\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}{s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}}_{\eqqcolon\,T_{3}}.
It is therefore sufficient to control each term separately.
Similar to the proof of Theorem 3 , the first term, T 1 T_{1} , after the summation is upper bounded by the score entropy loss
𝔼 x t , x ℓ ∼ q ← t , ℓ ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) ​ D ​ ( s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) , s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) ) \displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t},x_{\ell}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t,\ell}}\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})D(\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}),s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}))
≤ 𝔼 x ℓ ∼ q ← ℓ ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x ℓ ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) ​ D ​ ( s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) , s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) ) \displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{\ell}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{\ell}}\sum_{i\in m(x_{\ell})}\sum_{c\in[S]}s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})D(\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}),s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}))
= ℒ SE ​ ( T − ℓ , s ^ T − ℓ , s T − ℓ ) , \displaystyle\quad={\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{SE}}(T-\ell,\widehat{s}_{T-\ell},s_{T-\ell}),
and, by Assumption 1 ,
∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 ℒ SE ​ ( T − ℓ , s ^ T − ℓ , s T − ℓ ) ​ 𝑑 t ≤ ε score . \displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{SE}}(T-\ell,\widehat{s}_{T-\ell},s_{T-\ell})\mathrm{d}t\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}.
(101)
Now, we turn to controlling the terms T 2 T_{2} and T 3 T_{3} which require a different treatment than in the proof of Theorem 3 .
For the term T 2 T_{2} , we again apply the law of cosines with
α = s T − ℓ ( x ℓ ) , β = s T − t ( x ℓ ) , and γ = s T − t ( x t ) . \displaystyle\alpha=s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}),\qquad\beta=s_{T-t}(x_{\ell}),\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma=s_{T-t}(x_{t}).

s T − t ​ ( x t ) ​ D ​ ( s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) = s T − t ​ ( x t ) ​ D ​ ( s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ) ) ⏟ ≕ T 21 + s T − t ​ ( x t ) ​ D ​ ( s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) ⏟ ≕ T 22 + ( s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ) − s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) ​ s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) − s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ) s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ) ⏟ ≕ T 23 . \displaystyle\begin{aligned} s_{T-t}(x_{t})D(s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{t}))&=\underbrace{s_{T-t}(x_{t})D(s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{\ell}))}_{\eqqcolon\,T_{21}}\\
&\quad+\underbrace{s_{T-t}(x_{t})D(s_{T-t}(x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{t}))}_{\eqqcolon\,T_{22}}\\
&\qquad+\underbrace{(s_{T-t}(x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{t}))\frac{s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{\ell})}{s_{T-t}(x_{\ell})}}_{\eqqcolon\,T_{23}}.\end{aligned}

For T 21 T_{21} , using Equation 60 , observe that
D ⁡ ( s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ) ) \displaystyle D(s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{\ell}))
= e T − t − 1 e T − ℓ − 1 − 1 − log ⁡ e T − t − 1 e T − ℓ − 1 ≤ ( e T − ℓ − e T − t ) 2 2 ​ ( e T − ℓ − 1 ) ​ ( e T − t − 1 ) ≲ κ 2 , \displaystyle=\frac{e^{T-t}-1}{e^{T-\ell}-1}-1-\log\frac{e^{T-t}-1}{e^{T-\ell}-1}\leq\frac{(e^{T-\ell}-e^{T-t})^{2}}{2(e^{T-\ell}-1)(e^{T-t}-1)}\lesssim\kappa^{2},
where κ \kappa is a parameter of the step size schedule: t k + 1 − t k ≤ κ ​ min ⁡ ( 1 , T − t k + 1 ) t_{k+1}-t_{k}\leq\kappa\min(1,T-t_{k+1}) .
The total contribution of terms T 21 T_{21} is:
∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 𝔼 x t , x t k ∼ q ← t , t k ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ​ D ​ ( s T − t k ​ ( x t k ⊙ i c , x t k ) , s T − t ​ ( x t k ⊙ i c , x t k ) ) ​ 𝑑 t \displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\!\!{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t},x_{t_{k}}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t,t_{k}}}\!\!\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}\!s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})D(s_{T-t_{k}}(x_{t_{k}}\odot_{i}c,x_{t_{k}}),s_{T-t}(x_{t_{k}}\odot_{i}c,x_{t_{k}}))\mathrm{d}t
≲ κ 3 ​ ∑ k = 0 N − 1 𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) \displaystyle\quad\lesssim\kappa^{3}\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t}}\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})

as ∑ c ∈ [ S ] q t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c ) = q t ​ ( x t ) \sum_{c\in[S]}q_{t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c)=q_{t}(x_{t}) .

The term T 22 T_{22} is identical to the term T 2 T_{2} from the proof of Theorem 3 , thus we use Lemma 15 and obtain that after summation of over i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) i\in m(x_{t}) and c ∈ [ S ] c\in[S] :
∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 𝔼 x t k , x t ∼ q ← t k , t ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ​ D ​ ( s T − t ​ ( x t k ⊙ i c , x t k ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ) ​ 𝑑 t . \displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t_{k}},x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t_{k},t}}\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})D\left(s_{T-t}(x_{t_{k}}\odot_{i}c,x_{t_{k}}),s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})\right)\mathrm{d}t.
= ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 ∫ t k t e t − v ​ ℐ ​ ( T − v ) ​ 𝑑 v ​ 𝑑 t ≲ ∑ k = 0 N − 1 h k + 1 ​ ∫ T − t k + 1 T − t k ℐ ⁡ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t . \displaystyle\quad=\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\int_{t_{k}}^{t}e^{t-v}{\mathcal{I}}(T-v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k+1}\int_{T-t_{k+1}}^{T-t_{k}}{\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t.

Here, we invoke the assumption κ = O ⁡ ( 1 ) \kappa=O(1) and the non-negativity of conditional mutual information in the last inequality.
