To control T 23 T_{23} , observe that by Equation 60 , the score function satisfies the relation
s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) − s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ) s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ) = e T − t − e T − ℓ e T − ℓ − 1 , \displaystyle\frac{s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{\ell})}{s_{T-t}(x_{\ell})}=\frac{e^{T-t}-e^{T-\ell}}{e^{T-\ell}-1},
and importantly it does not depend on x ℓ x_{\ell} .
This implies that upon summation over i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) i\in m(x_{t}) and c ∈ [ S ] c\in[S] , the term T 23 T_{23} contributes zero, i.e.,
∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] ( s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) − s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ) ​ e T − t − e T − ℓ e T − ℓ − 1 \displaystyle\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}(s_{T-t}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t}))\frac{e^{T-t}-e^{T-\ell}}{e^{T-\ell}-1}
= e T − t − e T − ℓ e T − ℓ − 1 ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ( ∑ c ∈ [ S ] q t ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c ) q t ​ ( x ℓ ) − ∑ c ∈ [ S ] q t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c ) q t ​ ( x t ) ) \displaystyle\quad=\frac{e^{T-t}-e^{T-\ell}}{e^{T-\ell}-1}\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\left(\frac{\sum_{c\in[S]}q_{t}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c)}{q_{t}(x_{\ell})}-\frac{\sum_{c\in[S]}q_{t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c)}{q_{t}(x_{t})}\right)
= e T − t − e T − ℓ e T − ℓ − 1 ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ( 1 − 1 ) \displaystyle\quad=\frac{e^{T-t}-e^{T-\ell}}{e^{T-\ell}-1}\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\left(1-1\right)
= 0 . \displaystyle\quad=0.
Putting pieces together, we can conclude
∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 𝔼 x t k , x t ∼ q ← t k , t ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] T 2 ≤ κ 3 ​ N ​ d + ∑ k = 0 N − 1 h k + 1 ​ ∫ T − t k + 1 T − t k ℐ ⁡ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t . \displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\!\!{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t_{k}},x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{{t_{k},t}}}\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}T_{2}\leq\kappa^{3}Nd+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k+1}\int_{T-t_{k+1}}^{T-t_{k}}{\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t.
(104)
It therefore remains to control term T 3 T_{3} . Recall the definition
T 3 ≔ ( s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) − s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) ​ log ⁡ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) . \displaystyle T_{3}\coloneqq\left(s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{t})\right)\log\frac{\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}{s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}.
Crucially, unlike in the proof of Theorem 3 , we no longer have a martingale property for this term.
However, we can decompose
T 3 = ( s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) − s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ) ) ​ log ⁡ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) + ( s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ) − s T − t ​ ( x t ) ) ​ log ⁡ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) ⏟ contributes zero by Lemma 14 . \displaystyle T_{3}=\left(s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{\ell})\right)\log\frac{\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}{s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}+\underbrace{\left(s_{T-t}(x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{t})\right)\log\frac{\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}{s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}}_{\text{contributes zero by~\lx@cref{creftypecap~refnum}{lem:mask_mart}}}.
It remains to bound the first term, which can be written as
( s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) − s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ) ) ​ log ⁡ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) = e T − t − e T − ℓ e T − t − 1 ​ s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) ​ log ⁡ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ) . \displaystyle\left(s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{\ell})\right)\log\frac{\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}{s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}=\frac{e^{T-t}-e^{T-\ell}}{e^{T-t}-1}s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})\log\frac{\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}{s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell})}.
In view of the step size assumption t k + 1 − t k ≤ κ ​ min ⁡ ( 1 , T − t k + 1 ) t_{k+1}-t_{k}\leq\kappa\min(1,T-t_{k+1}) , it satisfies
| e T − t − e T − ℓ e T − t − 1 | ≲ κ . \displaystyle\left\lvert\frac{e^{T-t}-e^{T-\ell}}{e^{T-t}-1}\right\rvert\lesssim\kappa.
The total contribution of terms T 3 T_{3} can therefore be upper bounded by the following:
κ ​ ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ t k t k + 1 𝔼 x ℓ ∼ q ← ℓ ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x ℓ ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) ​ | log ⁡ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) | ​ 𝑑 t \displaystyle\kappa\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{\ell}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{\ell}}\sum_{i\in m(x_{\ell})}\sum_{c\in[S]}s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})\left\lvert\log\frac{\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})}{s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})}\right\rvert\mathrm{d}t
= κ ​ ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ( t k + 1 − t k ) ​ 𝔼 x ℓ ∼ q ← ℓ ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x ℓ ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) ​ | log ⁡ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) | . \displaystyle=\kappa\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}(t_{k+1}-t_{k}){\mathbb{E}}_{x_{\ell}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{\ell}}\sum_{i\in m(x_{\ell})}\sum_{c\in[S]}s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})\left\lvert\log\frac{\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})}{s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})}\right\rvert.
(105)
Collecting Eqns. ( 100 ), ( 101 ), ( 102 ), ( 106 ), and ( 105 ) proves
𝖪𝖫 ( q δ ∥ p T − δ ) ≲ ε score + e − T d log ( S ) + ∑ k = 0 N − 1 h k + 1 ∫ T − t k + 1 T − t k ℐ ( t ) d t + κ 3 N d + κ C , \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(q_{\delta}\,\|\,p_{T-\delta})\lesssim\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+e^{-T}d\log(S)+\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k+1}\int_{T-t_{k+1}}^{T-t_{k}}{\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t+\kappa^{3}Nd+\kappa C,
where
C ≔ ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ( t k + 1 − t k ) ​ 𝔼 x ℓ ∼ q ← ℓ ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x ℓ ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) ​ | log ⁡ s ^ T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) s T − ℓ ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) | . \displaystyle C\coloneqq\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}(t_{k+1}-t_{k}){\mathbb{E}}_{x_{\ell}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{\ell}}\sum_{i\in m(x_{\ell})}\sum_{c\in[S]}s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})\left\lvert\log\frac{\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})}{s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})}\right\rvert.
For our step size schedule, as in Corollary 2 , we upper bound
∑ k = 0 N − 1 h k + 1 ​ ∫ T − t k + 1 T − t k ℐ ⁡ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t ≤ κ ​ ∑ k = 0 N − 1 ∫ T − t k + 1 T − t k min ⁡ ( 1 , T − t k + 1 ) ​ ℐ ​ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t ≤ κ ​ ∫ δ T min ⁡ ( 1 , t ) ​ ℐ ​ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t ≤ κ ​ 𝒟 ​ ( q data ) . \displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}h_{k+1}\int_{T-t_{k+1}}^{T-t_{k}}{\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t\leq\kappa\sum_{k=0}^{N-1}\int_{T-t_{k+1}}^{T-t_{k}}\min(1,T-t_{k+1}){\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t\leq\kappa\int_{\delta}^{T}\min(1,t){\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t\leq\kappa{\mathcal{D}}(q_{\mathrm{data}}).
(106)
Plugging in the choices
κ = O ⁡ ( T + log ⁡ ( δ − 1 ) N ) \kappa=O\left(\frac{T+\log(\delta^{-1})}{N}\right) , T = O ⁡ ( log ⁡ ( ε − 1 ​ d ​ log ⁡ S ) ) T=O(\log(\varepsilon^{-1}d\log S)) , and
N = O ~ ​ ( 𝒟 ⁡ ( q data ) + C ε + d ε ) , \displaystyle N=\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{{\mathcal{D}}(q_{\mathrm{data}})+C}{\varepsilon}+\sqrt{\frac{d}{\varepsilon}}\right),
yields
𝖪𝖫 ( q δ ∥ p T − δ ) ≲ ε score + ε . \mathsf{KL}(q_{\delta}\|p_{T-\delta})\lesssim\varepsilon_{\mathrm{score}}+\varepsilon.
∎
Appendix E Proofs of the main lemmas
E.1 Characterization of ℬ ⁡ ( q data ) {\mathcal{B}}(q_{\mathrm{data}}) and 𝒞 ⁡ ( q data ) {\mathcal{C}}(q_{\mathrm{data}})
The characterization of ℬ ⁡ ( q data ) {\mathcal{B}}(q_{\mathrm{data}}) and 𝒞 ⁡ ( q data ) {\mathcal{C}}(q_{\mathrm{data}}) is summarized in the following lemma.
Lemma 16 .
Consider a masking noising process with initial distribution q 0 = q data q_{0}=q_{\mathrm{data}} . Let 𝒞 ⁡ ( q data ) {\mathcal{C}}(q_{\mathrm{data}}) and ℬ ⁡ ( q data ) {\mathcal{B}}(q_{\mathrm{data}}) be the total correlation and dual total correlation. Then,
ℬ ⁡ ( q data ) = ∫ 0 ∞ ℐ ⁡ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t and 𝒞 ⁡ ( q data ) = ∫ 0 ∞ ( e t − 1 ) ​ ℐ ​ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t . \displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}(q_{\mathrm{data}})=\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t\quad\text{and}\quad{\mathcal{C}}(q_{\mathrm{data}})=\int_{0}^{\infty}(e^{t}-1){\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t.
Consequently, 𝒟 ⁡ ( q data ) ≤ min ⁡ ( ℬ ⁡ ( q data ) , 𝒞 ⁡ ( q data ) ) {\mathcal{D}}(q_{\mathrm{data}})\leq\min({\mathcal{B}}(q_{\mathrm{data}}),{\mathcal{C}}(q_{\mathrm{data}})) .
Proof.
Let p ≡ p ⁡ ( t ) = e − t p\equiv p(t)=e^{-t} be the probability that at time t t a coordinate is unmasked and X ⁡ ( p ) ≡ ( X 1 , … , X d ) ≔ ( x t 1 , … , x t d ) X(p)\equiv(X_{1},\ldots,X_{d})\coloneqq(x_{t}^{1},\ldots,x_{t}^{d}) . We also denote X ℛ ≔ x t − ( i , j ) X_{{\mathcal{R}}}\coloneqq x_{t}^{-(i,j)} and X R ≔ ( X i ) i ∈ R X_{R}\coloneqq(X_{i})_{i\in R} for R ⊆ [ d ] R\subseteq[d] . With a slight abuse of notation we write ℐ ⁡ ( p ) ≔ ℐ ⁡ ( t ⁡ ( p ) ) {\mathcal{I}}(p)\coloneqq{\mathcal{I}}(t(p)) .
We have
ℐ ⁡ ( p ) ≔ ∑ i ≠ j I ⁡ ( X i ; X j ∣ X ℛ ) = ∑ i ≠ j p 2 ​ ∑ R ⊆ [ d ] ∖ { i , j } p | R | ​ ( 1 − p ) d − 2 − | R | ​ I ​ ( X i ; X j ∣ X R ) , {\mathcal{I}}(p)\coloneqq\sum_{i\neq j}\mathrm{I}(X_{i};X_{j}\mid X_{{\mathcal{R}}})=\sum_{i\neq j}p^{2}\sum_{R\subseteq[d]\setminus\{i,j\}}p^{\lvert R\rvert}(1-p)^{d-2-\lvert R\rvert}\mathrm{I}(X_{i};X_{j}\mid X_{R}),
where p 2 p^{2} appears since for the term I ⁡ ( X i ; X j ∣ X ℛ ) \mathrm{I}(X_{i};X_{j}\mid X_{\mathcal{R}}) to be non-zero, both X i X_{i} and X j X_{j} must be unmasked.
For i ∈ [ d ] i\in[d] , define
h i ​ ( p ) ≔ ∑ R ⊆ [ d ] ∖ { i } p | R | ​ ( 1 − p ) d − 1 − | R | ​ ℋ ​ ( X i ∣ X R ) , h_{i}(p)\coloneqq\sum_{R\subseteq[d]\setminus\{i\}}p^{\lvert R\rvert}(1-p)^{d-1-\lvert R\rvert}\mathcal{H}(X_{i}\mid X_{R}),
with
d ​ h i ​ ( p ) d ​ p \displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}h_{i}(p)}{\mathrm{d}p}
= ∑ R ⊆ [ d ] ∖ { i } [ | R | ​ p | R | − 1 ​ ( 1 − p ) d − 1 − | R | − ( d − 1 − | R | ) ​ p | R | ​ ( 1 − p ) d − 2 − | R | ] ​ ℋ ​ ( X i ∣ X R ) \displaystyle=\sum_{R\subseteq[d]\setminus\{i\}}\left[\lvert R\rvert p^{\lvert R\rvert-1}(1-p)^{d-1-\lvert R\rvert}-(d-1-\lvert R\rvert)p^{\lvert R\rvert}(1-p)^{d-2-\lvert R\rvert}\right]\mathcal{H}(X_{i}\mid X_{R})
= ∑ j ≠ i ∑ R ⊆ [ d ] ∖ { i , j } p | R | ​ ( 1 − p ) d − 2 − | R | ​ ( ℋ ⁡ ( X i ∣ X R ∪ { j } ) − ℋ ⁡ ( X i ∣ X R ) ) \displaystyle=\sum_{j\neq i}\sum_{R\subseteq[d]\setminus\{i,j\}}p^{\lvert R\rvert}(1-p)^{d-2-\lvert R\rvert}(\mathcal{H}(X_{i}\mid X_{R\cup\{j\}})-\mathcal{H}(X_{i}\mid X_{R}))
= − ∑ j ≠ i ∑ R ⊆ [ d ] ∖ { i , j } p | R | ( 1 − p ) d − 2 − | R | I ( X i ; X j ∣ X R ) . \displaystyle=-\sum_{j\neq i}\sum_{R\subseteq[d]\setminus\{i,j\}}p^{\lvert R\rvert}(1-p)^{d-2-\lvert R\rvert}\mathrm{I}(X_{i};X_{j}\mid X_{R}).
Therefore,
ℐ ⁡ ( p ) = ∑ i = 1 d p 2 ​ ( − d ​ h i ​ ( p ) d ​ p ) . {\mathcal{I}}(p)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}p^{2}\left(-\frac{\mathrm{d}h_{i}(p)}{\mathrm{d}p}\right).
Since p = e − t p=e^{-t} we have that d ​ t = − d ​ p p \mathrm{d}t=-\frac{\mathrm{d}p}{p} and we can write
∫ 0 ∞ ∑ i ≠ j I ⁡ ( X i ; X j ∣ X ℛ ) ​ 𝑑 t \displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\sum_{i\neq j}\mathrm{I}(X_{i};X_{j}\mid X_{{\mathcal{R}}})\mathrm{d}t
= ∫ 0 1 ∑ i = 1 d p ⁡ ( − d ​ h i ​ ( p ) d ​ p ) ​ d p = ∑ i = 1 d ( − p ​ h i ​ ( p ) ) | 0 1 + ∫ 0 1 ∑ i = 1 d h i ​ ( p ) ​ d p . \displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\sum_{i=1}^{d}p\left(-\frac{\mathrm{d}h_{i}(p)}{\mathrm{d}p}\right)\mathrm{d}p=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\big(-ph_{i}(p)\big)\Bigl|_{0}^{1}+\int_{0}^{1}\sum_{i=1}^{d}h_{i}(p)\mathrm{d}p.
Observe that
d ​ ℋ ​ ( X ⁡ ( p ) ) d ​ p = ∑ i = 1 d h i ​ ( p ) , \frac{\mathrm{d}\mathcal{H}(X(p))}{\mathrm{d}p}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}h_{i}(p),
therefore,
∫ 0 1 ∑ i = 1 d h i ​ ( p ) ​ 𝑑 p = ℋ ⁡ ( X ⁡ ( 1 ) ) − ℋ ⁡ ( X ⁡ ( 0 ) ) = ℋ ⁡ ( x 0 ) . \int_{0}^{1}\sum_{i=1}^{d}h_{i}(p)\mathrm{d}p=\mathcal{H}(X(1))-\mathcal{H}(X(0))=\mathcal{H}(x_{0}).
Since ∑ i = 1 d h i ​ ( 1 ) = ∑ i = 1 d ℋ ⁡ ( x 0 i ∣ x 0 − i ) \sum_{i=1}^{d}h_{i}(1)=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathcal{H}(x_{0}^{i}\mid x_{0}^{-i}) , we proved the first part:
∫ 0 ∞ ℐ ⁡ ( t ) ​ 𝑑 t = ℋ ⁡ ( x 0 ) − ∑ i = 1 d ℋ ⁡ ( x 0 i ∣ x 0 − i ) = ℬ ⁡ ( q 0 ) . \int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t=\mathcal{H}(x_{0})-\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathcal{H}(x_{0}^{i}\mid x_{0}^{-i})={\mathcal{B}}(q_{0}).
We proceed similarly for the total correlation:
∫ 0 ∞ ( e t − 1 ) ​ ∑ i ≠ j ℐ ⁡ ( t ) ​ d t = ∫ 0 1 ( 1 − p ) ​ ∑ i = 1 d ( − d ​ h i ​ ( p ) d ​ p ) ​ d p = − ( ∑ i = 1 d ( 1 − p ) ​ h i ​ ( p ) | 0 1 + ∫ 0 1 ∑ i = 1 d h i ​ ( p ) ​ d p ) \displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}(e^{t}-1)\sum_{i\neq j}{\mathcal{I}}(t)\mathrm{d}t=\int_{0}^{1}(1-p)\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(-\frac{\mathrm{d}h_{i}(p)}{\mathrm{d}p}\right)\mathrm{d}p=-\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}(1-p)h_{i}(p)\Bigl|_{0}^{1}+\int_{0}^{1}\sum_{i=1}^{d}h_{i}(p)\mathrm{d}p\right)
= ∑ i = 1 d ℋ ⁡ ( x 0 i ) − ℋ ⁡ ( x 0 ) = 𝒞 ⁡ ( q 0 ) . \displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathcal{H}(x_{0}^{i})-\mathcal{H}(x_{0})={\mathcal{C}}(q_{0}).
∎
E.2 Proof of Lemma 8
For any i ∈ [ d ] i\in[d] and c ∈ [ S ] c\in[S] , let us define
f i , c ( t , x t ) ≔ s T − t ( x t ⊕ i c , x t ) . \displaystyle f_{i,c}(t,x_{t})\coloneqq s_{T-t}(x_{t}\oplus_{i}c,x_{t}).
(107)
The following analysis holds for all i ∈ [ d ] i\in[d] and c ∈ [ S ] c\in[S] , so we safely omit the index i , c i,c in the following analysis, and write it as f ⁡ ( t , x t ) f(t,x_{t}) .
Consider the case that the backward process { x t } t ∈ [ 0 , T ] ∼ { q ← t } t ∈ [ 0 , T ] \{x_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}\sim\{\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]} , which is a Poisson jump process with generator L ← t \overset{\leftarrow}{L}_{t} such that
( L ← t ​ f ) ​ ( t , x ) \displaystyle\left(\overset{\leftarrow}{L}_{t}f\right)(t,x)
= ∑ y : d_H ⁡ ( y , x ) ≤ 1 Q T − t ( y , x ) s T − t ( y , x ) ( f ( t , y ) − f ( t , x ) ) \displaystyle=\sum_{y:\ham(y,x)\leq 1}Q_{T-t}(y,x)s_{T-t}(y,x)\big(f(t,y)-f(t,x)\big)
= 1 S ∑ y : d_H ⁡ ( y , x ) = 1 s T − t ( y , x ) ( f ( t , y ) − f ( t , x ) ) . \displaystyle=\frac{1}{S}\sum_{y:\ham(y,x)=1}s_{T-t}(y,x)\big(f(t,y)-f(t,x)\big).
By Itô’s formula for Poisson point process in Lemma 6 , f ⁡ ( t , x t ) f(t,x_{t}) satisfies the following stochastic differential equation: for 0 ≤ ℓ ≤ t < T 0\leq\ell\leq t<T ,
f ⁡ ( t , x t ) = f ⁡ ( ℓ , x ℓ ) + ∫ ℓ t [ ∂ t f ⁡ ( s , x s − ) + ( L ← s ​ f ) ​ ( s , x s − ) ] ​ 𝑑 s + M t , \displaystyle f(t,x_{t})=f(\ell,x_{\ell})+\int_{\ell}^{t}\left[\partial_{t}f(s,x_{s^{-}})+\left(\overset{\leftarrow}{L}_{s}f\right)(s,x_{s^{-}})\right]\mathrm{d}s+M_{t},
(108)
where x s − = lim u → s − x s x_{s-}=\lim_{u\to s^{-}}x_{s} , which exists for almost everywhere s ∈ [ 0 , T ) s\in[0,T) under the Lebesgue measure, since we have finite number of jumps almost surely. The compensation process { M u } u ∈ [ ℓ , t ] \{M_{u}\}_{u\in[\ell,t]} in Eqn. ( 108 ) is defined as
M u = ∑ y s : d_H ⁡ ( y s , x s ) = 1 ∫ ℓ u ( f ( s , y s ) − f ( s , x s ) ) ( d N s x s , y s − λ s x s , y s d s ) , \displaystyle M_{u}=\sum_{y_{s}:\ham(y_{s},x_{s})=1}\int_{\ell}^{u}\big(f(s,y_{s})-f(s,x_{s})\big)\big(\mathrm{d}N_{s}^{x_{s},y_{s}}-\lambda_{s}^{x_{s},y_{s}}\mathrm{d}s\big),
(109)
where N s x , y N_{s}^{x,y} is the counting process of jumps from x x to y y and we write the random counting measure as d ​ N s x , y \mathrm{d}N_{s}^{x,y} . We define λ s x , y = S − 1 s T − t ( y , x ) 𝕀 { x s − = x } \lambda^{x,y}_{s}=S^{-1}s_{T-t}(y,x){\mathbb{I}}\{x_{s^{-}}\!\!=x\} to be intensity of the process N s x , y N_{s}^{x,y} .
Since x s − = x s x_{s^{-}}=x_{s} almost everywhere s ∈ ( 0 , T ) s\in(0,T) due to the finite number of jumps for each path almost surely, we can rewrite Eqn. ( 108 ) as
f ⁡ ( t , x t ) − f ⁡ ( ℓ , x ℓ ) = ∫ ℓ t [ ∂ t f ⁡ ( s , x s ) + ( L ← s ​ f ) ​ ( s , x s ) ] ​ 𝑑 s + M t . \displaystyle f(t,x_{t})-f(\ell,x_{\ell})=\int_{\ell}^{t}\left[\partial_{t}f(s,x_{s})+\left(\overset{\leftarrow}{L}_{s}f\right)(s,x_{s})\right]\mathrm{d}s+M_{t}.
(110)
To further simplify the right hand side, we assert that
∂ t f ⁡ ( s , x s ) + ( L ← s ​ f ) ​ ( s , x s ) = 0 . \displaystyle\partial_{t}f(s,x_{s})+\left(\overset{\leftarrow}{L}_{s}f\right)(s,x_{s})=0.
(111)
In order to see this, first, recall the definition ( 107 ) and direct calculations give
∂ t f ⁡ ( s , x s ) + ( L ← s ​ f ) ​ ( s , x s ) \displaystyle\partial_{t}f(s,x_{s})+\left(\overset{\leftarrow}{L}_{s}f\right)(s,x_{s})
= ∂ ∂ s ​ ( q T − s ( x s ⊕ i c ) q T − s ​ ( x s ) ) \displaystyle=\frac{\partial}{\partial s}\left(\frac{q_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i}c)}{q_{T-s}(x_{s})}\right)
+ 1 S ∑ i ′ ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ′ ∈ [ S ] s T − s ( x s ⊕ i ′ c ′ , x s ) ( s T − s ( x s ⊕ i ′ c ′ ⊕ i c , x s ⊕ i ′ c ′ ) − s T − s ( x s ⊕ i c , x s ) ) \displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{S}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[d]}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[S]}s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime},x_{s})\Big(s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime}\oplus_{i}c,x_{s}\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime})-s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i}c,x_{s})\Big)
= ( a ) 1 S ∑ i ′ ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ′ ∈ [ S ] s T − s ( x s ⊕ i c , x s ) ( s T − s ( x s ⊕ i ′ c ′ , x s ) − s T − s ( x s ⊕ i c ⊕ i ′ c ′ , x s ⊕ i c ) ) \displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{=}\frac{1}{S}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[d]}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[S]}s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i}c,x_{s})\Big(s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime},x_{s})-s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i}c\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime},x_{s}\oplus_{i}c)\Big)
+ 1 S ∑ i ′ ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ′ ∈ [ S ] s T − s ( x s ⊕ i ′ c ′ , x s ) ( s T − s ( x s ⊕ i ′ c ′ ⊕ i c , x s ⊕ i ′ c ′ ) − s T − s ( x s ⊕ i c , x s ) ) \displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{S}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[d]}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[S]}s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime},x_{s})\Big(s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime}\oplus_{i}c,x_{s}\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime})-s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i}c,x_{s})\Big)
= 1 S ∑ i ′ ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ′ ∈ [ S ] ( s T − s ( x s ⊕ i c , x s ) s T − s ( x s ⊕ i ′ c ′ , x s ) − s T − s ( x s ⊕ i ′ c ′ , x s ) s T − s ( x s ⊕ i c , x s ) ) \displaystyle=\frac{1}{S}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[d]}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[S]}\Big(s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i}c,x_{s})s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime},x_{s})-s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime},x_{s})s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i}c,x_{s})\Big)
+ 1 S ∑ i ′ ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ′ ∈ [ S ] ( s T − s ( x s ⊕ i ′ c ′ , x s ) s T − s ( x s ⊕ i ′ c ′ ⊕ i c , x s ⊕ i ′ c ′ ) \displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{S}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[d]}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[S]}\Big(s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime},x_{s})s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime}\oplus_{i}c,x_{s}\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime})
− s T − s ( x s ⊕ i c , x s ) s T − s ( x s ⊕ i c ⊕ i ′ c ′ , x s ⊕ i c ) ) \displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i}c,x_{s})s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i}c\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime},x_{s}\oplus_{i}c)\Big)
= ( b ) 1 S ∑ i ′ ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ′ ∈ [ S ] ( s T − s ( x s ⊕ i ′ c ′ ⊕ i c , x s ) − s T − s ( x s ⊕ i c ⊕ i ′ c ′ , x s ) ) , \displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(b)}}{=}\frac{1}{S}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[d]}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[S]}\Big(s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime}\oplus_{i}c,x_{s})-s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i}c\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime},x_{s})\Big),
where in equality (a), we apply the Kolmogorov forward equation on q T − t q_{T-t} ; in equality (b), we use the fact that s T − t ​ ( x , y ) ​ s T − t ​ ( y , z ) = s T − t ​ ( x , z ) s_{T-t}(x,y)s_{T-t}(y,z)=s_{T-t}(x,z) for any x , y , z ∈ 𝒳 x,y,z\in{\mathcal{X}} .
It is direct to check that the ⊕ \oplus operators commute, i.e., for any x s ∈ 𝒳 x_{s}\in{\mathcal{X}} ,
x s ⊕ i ′ c ′ ⊕ i c = x s ⊕ i c ⊕ i ′ c ′ . \displaystyle x_{s}\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime}\oplus_{i}c=x_{s}\oplus_{i}c\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime}.
for i ≠ i ′ i\neq i^{\prime} , and the relation holds trivially when i = i ′ i=i^{\prime} .
This relation directly reveals that
∂ t f ⁡ ( s , x s ) + ( L ← s ​ f ) ​ ( s , x s ) \displaystyle\partial_{t}f(s,x_{s})+\left(\overset{\leftarrow}{L}_{s}f\right)(s,x_{s})
= 1 S ∑ i ′ ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ′ ∈ [ S ] ( s T − s ( x s ⊕ i ′ c ′ ⊕ i c , x s ) − s T − s ( x s ⊕ i c ⊕ i ′ c ′ , x s ) ) = 0 , \displaystyle=\frac{1}{S}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in[d]}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[S]}\Big(s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime}\oplus_{i}c,x_{s})-s_{T-s}(x_{s}\oplus_{i}c\oplus_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime},x_{s})\Big)=0,
which completes the proof of Eqn. ( 111 ).
Taking u = ℓ u=\ell in Eqn. ( 109 ), we have M ℓ = 0 M_{\ell}=0 almost surely, and M u M_{u} is a local martingale for u ∈ [ ℓ , t ] u\in[\ell,t] by definition.
Recalling Lemma 10 , we can bound
sup s ∈ [ ℓ , t ] sup x ∈ 𝒳 f ⁡ ( s , x ) ≤ log ⁡ ( S ) + max ⁡ { log ⁡ ( ( T − t ) − 1 ) , 0 } < ∞ . \displaystyle\sup_{s\in[\ell,t]}\sup_{x\in{\mathcal{X}}}f(s,x)\leq\log(S)+\max\Big\{\log\big((T-t)^{-1}\big),0\Big\}<\infty.
Similarly, for the intensity of the counting process, it satisfies
sup s ∈ [ ℓ , t ] sup x , y ∈ 𝒳 λ s x , y ≤ 1 S ​ s T − t ​ ( y , x ) ≤ 1 S ​ ( log ⁡ ( S ) + max ⁡ { log ⁡ ( ( T − t ) − 1 ) , 0 } ) < ∞ . \displaystyle\sup_{s\in[\ell,t]}\sup_{x,y\in{\mathcal{X}}}\lambda_{s}^{x,y}\leq\frac{1}{S}s_{T-t}(y,x)\leq\frac{1}{S}\left(\log(S)+\max\Big\{\log\big((T-t)^{-1}\big),0\Big\}\right)<\infty.
Now it is direct to check that
sup s ∈ [ ℓ , t ] 𝔼 ⁡ [ | M s | ] ≲ ( t − ℓ ) ​ d ​ ( S − 1 ) ⋅ sup s ∈ [ ℓ , t ] sup x , y ∈ 𝒳 [ f ⁡ ( s , x ) ⋅ λ s x , y ] < ∞ . \displaystyle\sup_{s\in[\ell,t]}\mathbb{E}[|M_{s}|]\lesssim(t-\ell)d(S-1)\cdot\sup_{s\in[\ell,t]}\sup_{x,y\in{\mathcal{X}}}\left[f(s,x)\cdot\lambda_{s}^{x,y}\right]<\infty.
As a result, we can conclude { M u } u ∈ [ ℓ , t ] \{M_{u}\}_{u\in[\ell,t]} is L 1 L^{1} and hence a martingale.
By the definition of the martingale, we arrive at
𝔼 q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ M t ] = M ℓ = 0 . \displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}[M_{t}]=M_{\ell}=0.
Returning to Eqn. ( 110 ), we obtain
𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ f ( t , x t ) − f ( ℓ , x ℓ ) ] = 𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ M t ] = 0 . \displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}\big[f(t,x_{t})-f(\ell,x_{\ell})\big]=\mathbb{E}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}[M_{t}]=0.
(112)
Thus, we conclude that
𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ ( s T − ℓ ( x ℓ ⊕ i c , x ℓ ) − s T − t ( x t ⊕ i c , x t ) ) log s ^ T − ℓ ( x ℓ ⊕ i c , x ℓ ) ] \displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t|\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}\Big[\big(s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\oplus_{i}c,x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{t}\oplus_{i}c,x_{t})\big)\log\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\oplus_{i}c,x_{\ell})\Big]
= 𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ f ( ℓ , x ℓ ) − f ( t , x t ) ] ⋅ log s ^ T − ℓ ( x ℓ ⊕ i c , x ℓ ) = 0 , \displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t|\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}\big[f(\ell,x_{\ell})-f(t,x_{t})\big]\cdot\log\widehat{s}_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\oplus_{i}c,x_{\ell})=0,
where we plug in Eqn. ( 112 ) in the last line.
∎
E.3 Proof of Lemma 9
The proof of Lemma 9 follows directly from exchanging the order of summation. Specifically, we can write
𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t [ ∑ y t : d_H ⁡ ( y t , x t ) = 1 h ( s T − t ( y t , x t ) ) ] \displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t}}\left[\sum_{y_{t}:\ham(y_{t},x_{t})=1}h\left(s_{T-t}(y_{t},x_{t})\right)\right]
= 𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t [ ∑ y t : d_H ⁡ ( y t , x t ) = 1 s T − t ( y t , x t ) log ( s T − t ( y t , x t ) ) − s T − t ( y t , x t ) + 1 ] \displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t}}\left[\sum_{y_{t}:\ham(y_{t},x_{t})=1}s_{T-t}(y_{t},x_{t})\log(s_{T-t}(y_{t},x_{t}))-s_{T-t}(y_{t},x_{t})+1\right]
= 𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t [ ∑ y t : d_H ⁡ ( y t , x t ) = 1 ( q T − t ​ ( y t ) q T − t ​ ( x t ) ) log ( s T − t ( y t , x t ) ) ] − 𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t [ ∑ y t : d_H ⁡ ( y t , x t ) = 1 ( q T − t ​ ( y t ) q T − t ​ ( x t ) ) ] + d ( S − 1 ) \displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t}}\left[\sum_{y_{t}:\ham(y_{t},x_{t})=1}\left(\frac{q_{T-t}(y_{t})}{q_{T-t}(x_{t})}\right)\log(s_{T-t}(y_{t},x_{t}))\right]-{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t}}\left[\sum_{y_{t}:\ham(y_{t},x_{t})=1}\left(\frac{q_{T-t}(y_{t})}{q_{T-t}(x_{t})}\right)\right]+d(S-1)
= ∑ x t ∈ [ S ] d ∑ y t : d_H ⁡ ( y t , x t ) = 1 q T − t ( y t ) log ( s T − t ( y t , x t ) ) − ∑ x t ∈ [ S ] d ∑ y t : d_H ⁡ ( y t , x t ) = 1 q T − t ( y t ) + d ( S − 1 ) \displaystyle=\sum_{x_{t}\in[S]^{d}}\sum_{y_{t}:\ham(y_{t},x_{t})=1}q_{T-t}(y_{t})\log(s_{T-t}(y_{t},x_{t}))-\sum_{x_{t}\in[S]^{d}}\sum_{y_{t}:\ham(y_{t},x_{t})=1}q_{T-t}(y_{t})+d(S-1)
= ( a ) − ∑ x t ∈ [ S ] d ∑ y t : d_H ⁡ ( y t , x t ) = 1 q T − t ( x t ) log ( s T − t ( y t , x t ) ) − ∑ y t ∈ [ S ] d ∑ x t : d_H ⁡ ( y t , x t ) = 1 q T − t ( y t ) + d ( S − 1 ) \displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{=}-\sum_{x_{t}\in[S]^{d}}\sum_{y_{t}:\ham(y_{t},x_{t})=1}q_{T-t}(x_{t})\log(s_{T-t}(y_{t},x_{t}))-\sum_{y_{t}\in[S]^{d}}\sum_{x_{t}:\ham(y_{t},x_{t})=1}q_{T-t}(y_{t})+d(S-1)
= − 𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t [ ∑ y t : d_H ⁡ ( y t , x t ) = 1 log ( s T − t ( y t , x t ) ) ] − d ( S − 1 ) + d ( S − 1 ) \displaystyle=-{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t}}\left[\sum_{y_{t}:\ham(y_{t},x_{t})=1}\log(s_{T-t}(y_{t},x_{t}))\right]-d(S-1)+d(S-1)
= 𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t [ ∑ y t : d_H ⁡ ( y t , x t ) = 1 − log ( s T − t ( y t , x t ) ) ] , \displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t}}\left[\sum_{y_{t}:\ham(y_{t},x_{t})=1}-\log\left(s_{T-t}(y_{t},x_{t})\right)\right],
where in equality (a), we switch the positions of x t x_{t} and y t y_{t} in the summations.
∎
E.4 Proof of Lemma 10
Lemma 10 is a direct consequence of Liang et al. (2025c, Lemma 2) .
Here, we present a simplified proof based on Proposition 6 . It can be easily checked that
α t = 1 − e − t 1 + ( S − 1 ) ​ e − t ∈ ( 0 , 1 ) . \displaystyle\alpha_{t}=\frac{1-e^{-t}}{1+(S-1)e^{-t}}\in(0,1).
By Eqn. ( 59 ), one has, for d_H ⁡ ( x , y ) = 1 \ham(x,y)=1 ,
s t ​ ( y , x ) = 𝔼 x 0 ∼ q 0 ​ α t d_H ⁡ ( y , x 0 ) 𝔼 x 0 ∼ q 0 ​ α t d_H ⁡ ( x , x 0 ) \displaystyle s_{t}(y,x)=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{0}\sim q_{0}}\alpha_{t}^{\ham(y,x_{0})}}{{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{0}\sim q_{0}}\alpha_{t}^{\ham{(x,x_{0})}}}
≤ α t − sup y , x , x 0 | d_H ( y , x 0 ) − d_H ( y , x 0 ) | \displaystyle\leq\alpha_{t}^{-\sup_{y,x,x_{0}}|\ham(y,x_{0})-\ham(y,x_{0})|}
= exp ( − log ( α t ) ⋅ sup y , x , x 0 | d_H ( y , x 0 ) − d_H ( y , x 0 ) | ) \displaystyle=\exp\left(-\log(\alpha_{t})\cdot\sup_{y,x,x_{0}}|\ham(y,x_{0})-\ham(y,x_{0})|\right)
≤ exp ( − log ( α t ) ⋅ sup y , x d_H ( y , x ) ) = exp ( − log ( α t ) ) . \displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\log(\alpha_{t})\cdot\sup_{y,x}\ham(y,x)\right)=\exp\big(-\log(\alpha_{t})\big).
With similar calculation, one can establish the reversed inequality
s t ​ ( y , x ) ≥ exp ⁡ ( log ⁡ ( α t ) ⋅ sup y , x d_H ⁡ ( y , x ) ) = exp ⁡ ( log ⁡ ( α t ) ) . \displaystyle s_{t}(y,x)\geq\exp\left(\log(\alpha_{t})\cdot\sup_{y,x}\ham(y,x)\right)=\exp\big(\log(\alpha_{t})\big).
As a result, we conclude
| log ⁡ ( s t ​ ( y , x ) ) | ≤ − log ⁡ ( α t ) ≲ log ⁡ ( S ) + max ⁡ { log ⁡ ( t − 1 ) , 0 } . \displaystyle|\log\big(s_{t}(y,x)\big)|\leq-\log(\alpha_{t})\lesssim\log(S)+\max\big\{\log(t^{-1}),0\big\}.
∎
E.5 Proof of Lemma 11
Let us start by proving the first equation, i.e.,
φ i , c ( t ) = 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ∥ ( N i , − c ) # q t ) . \displaystyle\varphi_{i,c}(t)=\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}\,\|\,(N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t}).
Recall the definition of φ i , c ​ ( t ) \varphi_{i,c}(t) that
φ i , c ​ ( t ) = 𝔼 x t ∼ q t ​ [ − log ⁡ ( q t ​ ( N i , c ​ ( x t ) ) q t ​ ( x t ) ) ] = ∑ x t ∈ 𝒳 q t ​ ( x ) ​ log ⁡ ( q t ​ ( x ) q t ​ ( N i , c ​ ( x t ) ) ) . \displaystyle\varphi_{i,c}(t)=\mathbb{E}_{x_{t}\sim q_{t}}\left[-\log\left(\frac{q_{t}\left(N_{i,c}(x_{t})\right)}{q_{t}(x_{t})}\right)\right]=\sum_{x_{t}\in{\mathcal{X}}}q_{t}(x)\log\left(\frac{q_{t}(x)}{q_{t}\left(N_{i,c}(x_{t})\right)}\right).
(113)
As in Eqn. ( 81 ), the pushforward measure satisfies
( N i , − c ) # ​ q t ​ ( x ) = q t ​ ( N i , c ​ ( x ) ) , for any ​ x ∈ 𝒳 . \displaystyle(N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t}(x)=q_{t}\left(N_{i,c}(x)\right),\text{ for any }x\in{\mathcal{X}}.
As such, we can express Eqn. ( 113 ) as
φ i , c ( t ) = ∑ x t ∈ 𝒳 q t ( x ) log ( q t ​ ( x ) ( N i , − c ) # ​ q t ​ ( x t ) ) = 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ∥ ( N i , − c ) # q t ) , \displaystyle\varphi_{i,c}(t)=\sum_{x_{t}\in{\mathcal{X}}}q_{t}(x)\log\left(\frac{q_{t}(x)}{(N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t}(x_{t})}\right)=\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}\,\|\,(N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t}),
which proves the first equation.
For the second relation, the definition of KL divergence gives
− ∂ ∂ t 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ∥ p 0 ) = − ∂ ∂ t ∑ x ∈ [ S ] d q t ( x ) log ( q t ( x ) ) \displaystyle-\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}\|p_{0})=-\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\sum_{x\in[S]^{d}}q_{t}(x)\log(q_{t}(x))
= − ∑ x ∈ [ S ] d d ​ q t ​ ( x ) d ​ t ( log ( q t ( x ) ) + 1 ) = − ∑ x ∈ [ S ] d d ​ q t ​ ( x ) d ​ t log ( q t ( x ) ) . \displaystyle=-\sum_{x\in[S]^{d}}\frac{\mathrm{d}q_{t}(x)}{\mathrm{d}t}\big(\log(q_{t}(x))+1\big)=-\sum_{x\in[S]^{d}}\frac{\mathrm{d}q_{t}(x)}{\mathrm{d}t}\log(q_{t}(x)).
(114)
Using the Kolmogorov forward equation for the forward noising process, we have
d ​ q t ​ ( x ) d ​ t = ∑ y ∈ 𝒳 Q ( x , y ) q t ( x ) = 1 S ∑ y : ( y , x ) = 1 q t ( y ) − d ⁡ ( S − 1 ) S q t ( x ) . \displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}q_{t}(x)}{\mathrm{d}t}=\sum_{y\in{\mathcal{X}}}Q(x,y)q_{t}(x)=\frac{1}{S}\sum_{y:\bm{(}y,x)=1}q_{t}(y)-\frac{d(S-1)}{S}q_{t}(x).
Plugging the equation above into Eqn. ( 114 ), we arrive at
− ∂ ∂ t 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ∥ p 0 ) \displaystyle-\frac{\partial}{\partial t}\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}\|p_{0})
= − ∑ x ∈ [ S ] d ( ∑ y : ( y , x ) = 1 ( 1 S q t ( y ) ) − d ⁡ ( S − 1 ) S q t ( x ) ) log ( q t ( x ) ) \displaystyle=-\sum_{x\in[S]^{d}}\left(\sum_{y:\bm{(}y,x)=1}\left(\frac{1}{S}q_{t}(y)\right)-\frac{d(S-1)}{S}q_{t}(x)\right)\log(q_{t}(x))
= − 1 S ∑ x ∈ [ S ] d ∑ y : ( y , x ) = 1 ( q t ( y ) − q t ( x ) ) log ( q t ( x ) ) \displaystyle=-\frac{1}{S}\sum_{x\in[S]^{d}}\sum_{y:\bm{(}y,x)=1}(q_{t}(y)-q_{t}(x))\log(q_{t}(x))
= − 1 S ∑ x ∈ [ S ] d ∑ y : ( y , x ) = 1 q t ( x ) ( log ( q t ( y ) ) − log ( q t ( x ) ) ) = φ ( t ) . \displaystyle=-\frac{1}{S}\sum_{x\in[S]^{d}}\sum_{y:\bm{(}y,x)=1}q_{t}(x)\big(\log(q_{t}(y))-\log(q_{t}(x))\big)=\varphi(t).
In addition, recall φ ( t ) = 1 / S ∑ i ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ∈ [ S ] φ i , c ( t ) \varphi(t)=1/S\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{c\in[S]}\varphi_{i,c}(t) . We reach
φ ( t ) = 1 S ∑ i ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ∈ [ S ] 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ∥ ( N i , − c ) # q t ) = 1 S ∑ i ∈ [ d ] ∑ c ∈ [ S ] 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ∥ ( N i , c ) # q t ) . \displaystyle\varphi(t)=\frac{1}{S}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{c\in[S]}\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}\,\|\,(N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t})=\frac{1}{S}\sum_{i\in[d]}\sum_{c\in[S]}\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}\,\|\,(N_{i,c})_{\#}q_{t}).
∎
E.6 Proof of Lemma 12
Let L L be the time-homogeneous infinitesimal generator of the forward process. Since each coordinate i ∈ [ d ] i\in[d] is updated independently in the forward process, we can write L = L i + L − i L=L_{i}+L_{-i} , where L i L_{i} only updates coordinate i i , and L − i L_{-i} updates all other coordinates. It direct to show that L i L_{i} and L − i L_{-i} commute, therefore, we have for any u ≥ 0 u\geq 0 ,
q t + u = q t ​ e u ​ L i ​ e u ​ L − i , ( N i , − c ) # ​ q t + u = ( ( N i , − c ) # ​ q t ) ​ e u ​ L i ​ e u ​ L − i , \displaystyle q_{t+u}=q_{t}e^{uL_{i}}e^{uL_{-i}},\quad(N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t+u}=((N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t})e^{uL_{i}}e^{uL_{-i}},
where the second equation is due to the operator N i , − c N_{i,-c} commutes with the semigroup { e u ​ L } u ≥ 0 \{e^{uL}\}_{u\geq 0} . With this formulation, we reach
φ i , c ( t + u ) = 𝖪𝖫 ( q t + u ∥ ( N i , − c ) # q t + u ) ≤ 𝖪𝖫 ( q t e u ​ L i ∥ ( ( N i , − c ) # q t ) e u ​ L i ) , \displaystyle\varphi_{i,c}(t+u)=\mathsf{KL}(q_{t+u}\,\|\,(N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t+u})\leq\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}e^{uL_{i}}\,\|\,((N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t})e^{uL_{i}}),
(115)
where in the last inequality, we apply the weak data processing inequality for KL divergence.
Since both N i , − c N_{i,-c} and L i L_{i} only operate on the coordinate i i , we arrive at the decomposition
𝖪𝖫 ( q t e u ​ L i ∥ ( ( N i , − c ) # q t ) e u ​ L i ) = 𝔼 x − i ∼ ( q t ) − i [ 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ( ⋅ | x − i ) e u ​ L i ∥ ( ( N i , − c ) # q t ( ⋅ | x − i ) ) e u ​ L i ) ] , \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}e^{uL_{i}}\,\|\,((N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t})e^{uL_{i}})={\mathbb{E}}_{x^{-i}\sim(q_{t})^{-i}}\left[\mathsf{KL}\big(q_{t}(\cdot|x^{-i})e^{uL_{i}}\,\|\,((N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t}(\cdot|x^{-i}))e^{uL_{i}}\big)\right],
(116)
where ( q t ) − i (q_{t})^{-i} is the marginal distribution of q t q_{t} with coordinate i i excluded.
Define K u K_{u} to be the transition kernel on [ S ] × [ S ] [S]\times[S] induced by e u ​ L i e^{uL_{i}} .
It is shown that
K u ​ ( v 1 , v 2 ) = { 1 S + ( 1 − 1 S ) ​ e − u if ​ v 1 = v 2 ; 1 S ​ ( 1 − e − u ) if ​ v 1 ≠ v 2 . \displaystyle K_{u}(v_{1},v_{2})=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{S}+(1-\frac{1}{S})e^{-u}\quad&\text{if }v_{1}=v_{2};\\
\frac{1}{S}(1-e^{-u})\quad&\text{if }v_{1}\neq v_{2}.\end{cases}
It can be directly checked that K u K_{u} is a S S -ary symmetric channel with noise scale σ u = ( 1 − S − 1 ) ​ ( 1 − e − u ) \sigma_{u}=(1-S^{-1})(1-e^{-u}) .
By Makur and Polyanskiy (2018, Proposition 12) , a strong data processing inequality holds for the channel K u K_{u} , i.e., for any distribution p , q p,q supported on [ S ] [S] ,
𝖪𝖫 ( p e u ​ L i ∥ q e u ​ L i ) ≤ η 𝖪𝖫 ( K u ) 𝖪𝖫 ( p ∥ q ) , \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(pe^{uL_{i}}\,\|\,qe^{uL_{i}})\leq\eta_{\mathsf{KL}}(K_{u})\mathsf{KL}(p\,\|\,q),
where η 𝖪𝖫 ​ ( K u ) \eta_{\mathsf{KL}}(K_{u}) satisfies
η 𝖪𝖫 ​ ( K u ) ≤ | 1 − σ u − σ u S − 1 | = 1 − S S − 1 ​ ( 1 − S − 1 ) ​ ( 1 − e − u ) = e − u . \displaystyle\eta_{\mathsf{KL}}(K_{u})\leq\bigg|1-\sigma_{u}-\frac{\sigma_{u}}{S-1}\bigg|=1-\frac{S}{S-1}(1-S^{-1})(1-e^{-u})=e^{-u}.
Taking this strong data processing inequality with Eqn. ( 116 ) yields
𝖪𝖫 ( q t e u ​ L i ∥ ( ( N i , − c ) # q t ) e u ​ L i ) \displaystyle\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}e^{uL_{i}}\|((N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t})e^{uL_{i}})
= 𝔼 x − i ∼ ( q t ) − i [ 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ( ⋅ | x − i ) e u ​ L i ∥ ( ( N i , − c ) # q t ( ⋅ | x − i ) ) e u ​ L i ) ] \displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{x^{-i}\sim(q_{t})^{-i}}\left[\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}(\cdot|x^{-i})e^{uL_{i}}\,\|\,((N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t}(\cdot|x^{-i}))e^{uL_{i}})\right]
≤ 𝔼 x − i ∼ ( q t ) − i [ e − u 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ( ⋅ | x − i ) ∥ ( ( N i , − c ) # q t ( ⋅ | x − i ) ) ] \displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{-i}\sim(q_{t})^{-i}}\left[e^{-u}\,\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}(\cdot|x^{-i})\,\|\,((N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t}(\cdot|x_{-i}))\right]
≤ e − u 𝔼 x − i ∼ ( q t ) − i [ 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ( ⋅ | x − i ) ∥ ( ( N i , − c ) # q t ( ⋅ | x − i ) ) ] \displaystyle\leq e^{-u}\,{\mathbb{E}}_{x^{-i}\sim(q_{t})^{-i}}\left[\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}(\cdot|x^{-i})\,\|\,((N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t}(\cdot|x^{-i}))\right]
= e − u 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ∥ ( N i , − c ) # q t ) . \displaystyle=e^{-u}\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}\,\|\,(N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t}).
Then, by Eqn. ( 115 ), we have
φ i , c ( t + u ) ≤ 𝖪𝖫 ( q t e u ​ L i ∥ ( ( N i , − c ) # q t ) e u ​ L i ) ≤ e − u 𝖪𝖫 ( q t ∥ ( N i , − c ) # q t ) = e − u φ i , c ( t ) , \displaystyle\varphi_{i,c}(t+u)\leq\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}e^{uL_{i}}\,\|\,((N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t})e^{uL_{i}})\leq e^{-u}\mathsf{KL}(q_{t}\,\|\,(N_{i,-c})_{\#}q_{t})=e^{-u}\varphi_{i,c}(t),
which holds for any u ≥ 0 u\geq 0 .
Therefore, the derivative can be bound as
φ i , c ′ ​ ( t ) = lim u → 0 + φ i , c ​ ( t + u ) − φ i , c ​ ( t ) u ≤ lim u → 0 + e − u u ​ φ i , c ​ ( t ) = − φ i , c ​ ( t ) , \displaystyle\varphi_{i,c}^{\prime}(t)=\lim_{u\to 0^{+}}\frac{\varphi_{i,c}(t+u)-\varphi_{i,c}(t)}{u}\leq\lim_{u\to 0^{+}}\frac{e^{-u}}{u}\varphi_{i,c}(t)=-\varphi_{i,c}(t),
which induces the target result
− φ i , c ′ ​ ( t ) ≥ φ i , c ​ ( t ) . \displaystyle-\varphi_{i,c}^{\prime}(t)\geq\varphi_{i,c}(t).
∎
E.7 Proof of Lemma 14
The proof follows from ( Conforti et al., 2025 , Lemma 5.2.2) . We add the argument below for completeness.
Let us define
f ( t , x t ) ≔ s T − t ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) 𝕀 { i ∈ m ( x t ) } , \displaystyle f(t,x_{t})\coloneqq s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t}){\mathbb{I}}\{i\in m(x_{t})\},
where the dependence on i i and c c is omitted for simplicity.
In view of Lemma 6 , for 0 ≤ ℓ ≤ t < T 0\leq\ell\leq t<T , we can write
f ⁡ ( t , x t ) = f ⁡ ( ℓ , x ℓ ) + ∫ ℓ t [ ∂ t f ⁡ ( s , x s ) + ( L ← s ​ f ) ​ ( s , x s ) ] ​ 𝑑 s + M t , \displaystyle f(t,x_{t})=f(\ell,x_{\ell})+\int_{\ell}^{t}\left[\partial_{t}f(s,x_{s})+(\overset{\leftarrow}{L}_{s}f)(s,x_{s})\right]\mathrm{d}s+M_{t},
with generator { L ← s } s ∈ [ ℓ , t ] \{\overset{\leftarrow}{L}_{s}\}_{s\in[\ell,t]} as follows
( L ← s ​ f ) ​ ( s , x ) \displaystyle\left(\overset{\leftarrow}{L}_{s}f\right)(s,x)
= ∑ y ≠ x Q T − s ​ ( y , x ) ​ s T − s ​ ( y , x ) ​ ( f ⁡ ( s , y ) − f ⁡ ( s , x ) ) \displaystyle=\sum_{y\neq x}Q_{T-s}(y,x)s_{T-s}(y,x)\big(f(s,y)-f(s,x)\big)
= ∑ i ′ ∈ m ⁡ ( x ) ∑ c ′ ∈ [ S ] s T − s ​ ( x ⊙ i ′ c ′ , x ) ​ ( f ⁡ ( s , x ⊙ i ′ c ′ ) − f ⁡ ( s , x ) ) , \displaystyle=\sum_{i^{\prime}\in m(x)}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[S]}s_{T-s}(x\odot_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime},x)\big(f(s,x\odot_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime})-f(s,x)\big),
and the compensation process { M u } u ∈ [ ℓ , t ] \{M_{u}\}_{u\in[\ell,t]} defined as
M u = ∫ ℓ u ∑ i ′ ∈ m ⁡ ( x s ) ∑ c ′ ∈ [ S ] ( f ⁡ ( s , x s ⊙ i ′ c ′ ) − f ⁡ ( s , x s ) ) ​ ( d ​ N s x s , x s ⊙ i ′ c ′ − λ s x s , x s ⊙ i ′ c ′ ​ 𝑑 s ) . \displaystyle M_{u}=\int_{\ell}^{u}\sum_{i^{\prime}\in m(x_{s})}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[S]}\big(f(s,x_{s}\odot_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime})-f(s,x_{s})\big)\big(\mathrm{d}N_{s}^{x_{s},x_{s}\odot_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime}}-\lambda_{s}^{x_{s},x_{s}\odot_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}s\big).
With similar argument as in the proof of Lemma 8 , one has
𝔼 q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ M t ] = 0 , \mathbb{E}_{\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}[M_{t}]=0, which leads to
𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ f ( t , x t ) − f ( ℓ , x ℓ ) ] = 𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ ∫ ℓ t ( ∂ t f ( s , x s ) + ( L ← s f ) ( s , x s ) ) d s ] . \displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}[f(t,x_{t})-f(\ell,x_{\ell})]={\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}\left[\int_{\ell}^{t}\big(\partial_{t}f(s,x_{s})+(\overset{\leftarrow}{L}_{s}f)(s,x_{s})\big)\mathrm{d}s\right].
Taking derivative with respect to t t on both side, we arrive at
d d ​ t 𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ f ( t , x t ) ] = 𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ ∂ t f ( t , x t ) + ( L ← t f ) ( t , x t ) ] . \displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}[f(t,x_{t})]={\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}\left[\partial_{t}f(t,x_{t})+(\overset{\leftarrow}{L}_{t}f)(t,x_{t})\right].
Now let us consider each term on the right hand side above separately. By Proposition 6 , it obeys that
s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) = 1 e T − t − 1 ​ q 0 ​ ( x t ⊙ i c ) q 0 ​ ( x t ) , s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})=\frac{1}{e^{T-t}-1}\frac{q_{0}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c)}{q_{0}(x_{t})},
and we have
∂ ∂ t ​ f ​ ( t , x t ) = e T − t e T − t − 1 ​ f ​ ( t , x t ) . \displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}f(t,x_{t})=\frac{e^{T-t}}{e^{T-t}-1}f(t,x_{t}).
Next, direct calculations yield
( L ← t ​ f ) ​ ( t , x t ) \displaystyle(\overset{\leftarrow}{L}_{t}f)(t,x_{t})
= ∑ i ′ ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ′ ∈ [ S ] s T − t ( x t ⊙ i ′ c ′ , x t ) ( s T − t ( x t ⊙ i c ⊙ i ′ c ′ , x t ⊙ i ′ c ′ ) 𝕀 { i ∈ m ( x t ⊙ i ′ c ′ ) } \displaystyle=\sum_{i^{\prime}\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[S]}s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime},x_{t})\Bigl(s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c\odot_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime},x_{t}\odot_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime}){\mathbb{I}}\{i\in m(x_{t}\odot_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime})\}
− s T − t ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) 𝕀 { i ∈ m ( x t ) } ) \displaystyle\hskip 100.00015pt-s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t}){\mathbb{I}}\{i\in m(x_{t})\}\Bigr)
= 1 e T − t − 1 ​ f ​ ( t , x t ) ​ ( ∑ i ′ ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∖ { i } ∑ c ′ ∈ [ S ] q 0 ( x t ⊙ i c ⊙ i ′ c ′ ) q 0 ​ ( x t ⊙ i c ) − ∑ i ′ ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ′ ∈ [ S ] q 0 ​ ( x t ⊙ i ′ c ′ ) q 0 ​ ( x t ) ) \displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{e^{T-t}-1}f(t,x_{t})\left(\sum_{i^{\prime}\in m(x_{t})\setminus\{i\}}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[S]}\frac{q_{0}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c\odot_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime})}{q_{0}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c)}-\sum_{i^{\prime}\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c^{\prime}\in[S]}\frac{q_{0}(x_{t}\odot_{i^{\prime}}c^{\prime})}{q_{0}(x_{t})}\right)
= 1 e T − t − 1 ​ f ​ ( t , x t ) ​ ( | m ⁡ ( x t ) ∖ { i } | − | m ⁡ ( x t ) | ) = − 1 e T − t − 1 ​ f ​ ( t , x t ) . \displaystyle\quad=\frac{1}{e^{T-t}-1}f(t,x_{t})(|m(x_{t})\setminus\{i\}|-|m(x_{t})|)=-\frac{1}{e^{T-t}-1}f(t,x_{t}).
Putting everything together leads to
d d ​ t 𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ f ( t , x t ) ] = 𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ f ( t , x t ) ] , \displaystyle\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}[f(t,x_{t})]={\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}[f(t,x_{t})],
and therefore,
𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ f ( t , x t ) ] = e t − ℓ ⋅ f ( ℓ , x ℓ ) . \displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}[f(t,x_{t})]=e^{t-\ell}\cdot f(\ell,x_{\ell}).
Finally, in view of the relation Pr ⁡ ( x t i = MASK ∣ x ℓ i = MASK ) = 1 − e T − t 1 − e T − ℓ \Pr(x_{t}^{i}=\mathrm{MASK}\mid x_{\ell}^{i}=\mathrm{MASK})=\frac{1-e^{T-t}}{1-e^{T-\ell}} , we conclude the following
𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ s T − t ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) 𝕀 { i ∈ m ( x t ) } ] = e t − ℓ ⋅ s T − ℓ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) 𝕀 { i ∈ m ( x ℓ ) } = 𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ s T − t ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) 𝕀 { i ∈ m ( x t ) } ] , \displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}\big[s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})\,{\mathbb{I}}\{i\in m(x_{t})\}\big]&=e^{t-\ell}\cdot s_{T-\ell}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}){\mathbb{I}}\{i\in m(x_{\ell})\}\\
&={\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}\big[s_{T-t}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})\,{\mathbb{I}}\{i\in m(x_{t})\}\big],\end{aligned}
which completes the proof of the desired result.
∎
Appendix F Proofs of the auxiliary lemmas
F.1 Proof of Lemma 2
For a continuous random variable U U in ℝ d \mathbb{R}^{d} with density function p U p_{U} with respect to Lebesgue measure, define the differential entropy of U U as
ℋ diff ( U ) = − ∫ ℝ d p U log ( p U ) d x , \displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(U)=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}p_{U}\log(p_{U})\mathrm{d}x,
(117)
where we adopt the convention 0 ​ log ⁡ ( 0 ) = 0 0\log(0)=0 again.
By definition of mutual information, we have
I ⁡ ( W , W + ε noise ) \displaystyle I(W;W+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}})
= ℋ diff ​ ( W + ε noise ) − ℋ diff ​ ( W + ε noise ∣ W ) \displaystyle={\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(W+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}})-{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(W+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}}\mid W)
= ( a ) ​ ℋ diff ​ ( W + ε noise ) − 𝔼 w ​ [ ℋ diff ​ ( w + ε noise ∣ W = w ) ] \displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(a)}}{=}{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(W+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}})-\mathbb{E}_{w}\big[{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(w+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}}\mid W=w)\big]
= ( b ) ​ ℋ diff ​ ( W + ε noise ) − 𝔼 w ​ [ ℋ diff ​ ( ε noise ∣ W = w ) ] \displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(b)}}{=}{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(W+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}})-\mathbb{E}_{w}\big[{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}}\mid W=w)\big]
= ( c ) ​ ℋ diff ​ ( W + ε noise ) − ℋ diff ​ ( ε noise ) , \displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{(c)}}{=}{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(W+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}})-{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}}),
(118)
where in (a), we use the chain rule of differential entropy; in (b), we apply the translation invariance property, i.e., ℋ diff ​ ( U ) = ℋ diff ​ ( c 0 + U ) {\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(U)={\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(c_{0}+U) for any constant c 0 c_{0} ; in (c), we use the condition that ε noise ⟂ ⟂ W \varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}}\perp\!\!\!\perp W .
Denote the Gaussian density function with mean 0 0 and variance σ 2 ​ I d \sigma^{2}I_{d} as ϕ σ ​ ( ⋅ ) \phi_{\sigma}(\cdot) .
Since ε noise ∼ 𝒩 ⁡ ( 0 , σ 2 ​ I d ) \varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}}\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,\sigma^{2}I_{d}) , we can compute with Equation 117 that
ℋ diff ​ ( ε noise ) \displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}})
= − ∫ ℝ d ϕ σ ( x ) log ( ϕ σ ( x ) ) d x \displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\phi_{\sigma}(x)\log\big(\phi_{\sigma}(x)\big)\mathrm{d}x
= − ∫ ℝ d ϕ σ ( x ) ( − d 2 log ( 2 π σ 2 ) − ‖ x ‖ 2 2 2 ​ σ 2 ) d x \displaystyle=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\phi_{\sigma}(x)\left(-\frac{d}{2}\log(2\pi\sigma^{2})-\frac{\|x\|_{2}^{2}}{2\sigma^{2}}\right)\mathrm{d}x
= d 2 ​ log ⁡ ( 2 ​ π ​ σ 2 ) + 𝔼 ⁡ [ ‖ ε noise ‖ 2 2 ] 2 ​ σ 2 \displaystyle=\frac{d}{2}\log(2\pi\sigma^{2})+\frac{\mathbb{E}[\|\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}}\|_{2}^{2}]}{2\sigma^{2}}
= d 2 ​ log ⁡ ( 2 ​ π ​ e ​ σ 2 ) , \displaystyle=\frac{d}{2}\log(2\pi e\sigma^{2}),
(119)
where ∥ ⋅ ∥ 2 \|\cdot\|_{2} is the Euclidean norm in ℝ d \mathbb{R}^{d} .
For ℋ diff ​ ( W + ε noise ) {\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(W+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}}) , notice that
Var ⁡ [ W + ε noise ] = Var ⁡ [ W ] + Var ⁡ [ ε noise ] + 2 ​ Cov ​ [ W , ε noise ] = Var ⁡ [ W ] + σ 2 ​ I d . \displaystyle\mathrm{Var}\big[W+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}}\big]=\mathrm{Var}[W]+\mathrm{Var}[\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}}]+2\,\mathrm{Cov}\big[W,\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}}\big]=\mathrm{Var}[W]+\sigma^{2}I_{d}.
By Cover (1999, Page 255) , for distributions with the same finite variance, ℋ diff {\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}} is maximized at the centered Gaussian random variable.
Therefore, we have
ℋ diff ​ ( W + ε noise ) \displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(W+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}})
≤ ℋ diff ​ ( 𝒩 ⁡ ( 0 , Var ⁡ [ W ] + σ 2 ​ I d ) ) = d 2 ​ log ⁡ ( 2 ​ π ​ e ) + 1 2 ​ log ⁡ ( det ⁡ ( Var ⁡ [ W ] + σ 2 ​ I d ) ) , \displaystyle\leq{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}\Big({\mathcal{N}}\big(0,\mathrm{Var}[W]+\sigma^{2}I_{d}\big)\Big)=\frac{d}{2}\log(2\pi e)+\frac{1}{2}\log\left(\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{Var}[W]+\sigma^{2}I_{d}\big)\right),
where det ⁡ ( ⋅ ) \mathrm{det}(\cdot) is the determinant of matrices, and the calculation is the same as in Equation 119 .
Since Var ⁡ [ W ] \mathrm{Var}[W] is a positive semidefinite matrix, we can apply the matrix inequality that
log ⁡ ( det ⁡ ( Var ⁡ [ W ] + σ 2 ​ I d ) ) \displaystyle\log\left(\mathrm{det}\big(\mathrm{Var}[W]+\sigma^{2}I_{d}\big)\right)
= d ​ log ⁡ ( σ 2 ) + log ⁡ ( det ⁡ ( I d + Var ⁡ [ W / σ 2 ] ) ) \displaystyle=d\log(\sigma^{2})+\log\left(\mathrm{det}\big(I_{d}+\mathrm{Var}[W/\sigma^{2}]\big)\right)
≤ d ​ log ⁡ ( σ 2 ) + Tr ⁡ ( Var ⁡ [ W / σ 2 ] ) \displaystyle\leq d\log(\sigma^{2})+\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathrm{Var}[W/\sigma^{2}]\right)
= d ​ log ⁡ ( σ 2 ) + Tr ⁡ ( Var ⁡ [ W ] ) σ 2 , \displaystyle=d\log(\sigma^{2})+\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathrm{Var}[W]\right)}{\sigma^{2}},
which leads to
ℋ diff ​ ( W + ε noise ) \displaystyle{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathrm{diff}}(W+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}})
≤ d 2 ​ log ⁡ ( 2 ​ π ​ e ​ σ 2 ) + Tr ⁡ ( Var ⁡ [ W ] ) 2 ​ σ 2 . \displaystyle\leq\frac{d}{2}\log(2\pi e\sigma^{2})+\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathrm{Var}[W]\right)}{2\sigma^{2}}.
(120)
Plugging Eqns. ( 119 ) and ( 120 ) into Equation 118 , we conclude that
I ⁡ ( W , W + ε noise ) ≤ d 2 ​ log ⁡ ( 2 ​ π ​ e ​ σ 2 ) + Tr ⁡ ( Var ⁡ [ W ] ) 2 ​ σ 2 − d 2 ​ log ⁡ ( 2 ​ π ​ e ​ σ 2 ) = Tr ⁡ ( Var ⁡ [ W ] ) 2 ​ σ 2 . \displaystyle I(W;W+\varepsilon_{\mathrm{noise}})\leq\frac{d}{2}\log(2\pi e\sigma^{2})+\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathrm{Var}[W]\right)}{2\sigma^{2}}-\frac{d}{2}\log(2\pi e\sigma^{2})=\frac{\mathrm{Tr}\left(\mathrm{Var}[W]\right)}{2\sigma^{2}}.
∎
F.2 Proof of Lemma 3
For X ∼ Bin ⁡ ( n , 1 / 2 ) X\sim\mathrm{Bin}(n,1/2) , its pmf is given by
ℙ ⁡ ( X = x ) = ( n x ) ​ ( 1 2 ) n ∝ ( n x ) . \displaystyle\mathbb{P}(X=x)={n\choose x}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{n}\propto{n\choose x}.
Notice that our desired bound is equivalent to the following equation:
∑ x : x mod 2 = 0 ( n x ) = ∑ x : x mod 2 = 1 ( n x ) , \displaystyle\sum_{x:x\bmod 2=0}{n\choose x}=\sum_{x:x\bmod 2=1}{n\choose x},
which follows from the binomial theorem for 0 = ( 1 − 1 ) n 0=(1-1)^{n} . ∎
F.3 Proof of Lemma 13
The CTMC ( 88 ) in the lemma statement can be decomposed into d d independent CTMCs for each coordinate. For coordinates i i such that x t k i ≠ MASK x_{t_{k}}^{i}\neq\mathrm{MASK} clearly neither Equation 88 nor Algorithm 1 makes a change. Next, we fix i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t k ) i\in m(x_{t_{k}}) .
First, we compute the probability that the i i -th coordinate remains masked for y t k + 1 y_{t_{k+1}} :
Pr ⁡ ( y t k + 1 i = MASK ∣ y t k ) \displaystyle\Pr(y_{t_{k+1}}^{i}=\mathrm{MASK}\mid y_{t_{k}})
= exp ( ∫ t k t k + 1 ( − ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s ^ T − t k ( y t k ⊙ i c , y t k ) e T − t k − 1 e T − t − 1 ) d t ) \displaystyle=\exp\left(\int_{t_{k}}^{t_{k+1}}\left(-\sum_{c\in[S]}\widehat{s}_{T-t_{k}}(y_{t_{k}}\odot_{i}c,y_{t_{k}})\frac{e^{T-t_{k}}-1}{e^{T-t}-1}\right)\mathrm{d}t\right)
= exp ⁡ ( Q ^ k i ​ ( MASK ) ​ Δ k ) \displaystyle=\exp(\widehat{Q}_{k}^{i}(\mathrm{MASK})\Delta_{k})
= 𝒫 k , \displaystyle={\mathcal{P}}_{k},
where Q ^ k i ​ ( MASK ) \widehat{Q}_{k}^{i}(\mathrm{MASK}) , Δ k \Delta_{k} , and 𝒫 k {\mathcal{P}}_{k} are defined in Algorithm 1 . Next, for c ∈ [ S ] c\in[S] we can write
Pr ⁡ ( y t k + 1 i = c ∣ x t k ) = Pr ⁡ ( x t k + 1 i = c ∣ x t k ​ and ​ x t k + 1 i ≠ MASK ) ​ ( 1 − 𝒫 k ) . \Pr(y_{t_{k+1}}^{i}=c\mid x_{t_{k}})=\Pr(x_{t_{k+1}}^{i}=c\mid x_{t_{k}}\text{ and }x_{t_{k+1}}^{i}\neq\mathrm{MASK})(1-{\mathcal{P}}_{k}).
Since for any t ∈ [ t k , t k + 1 ) t\in[t_{k},t_{k+1}) the rates Q ^ t ​ ( x , x ⊙ i c ) \widehat{Q}_{t}(x,x\odot_{i}c) are proportional to Q ^ k i ​ ( c ) \widehat{Q}_{k}^{i}(c) , we get that
Pr ⁡ ( y t k + 1 i = c ∣ x t k ​ and ​ y t k + 1 i ≠ MASK ) = Q ^ k i ​ ( c ) ∑ b ∈ [ S ] Q ^ k i ​ ( b ) , \Pr(y_{t_{k+1}}^{i}=c\mid x_{t_{k}}\text{ and }y_{t_{k+1}}^{i}\neq\mathrm{MASK})=\frac{\widehat{Q}_{k}^{i}(c)}{\sum_{b\in[S]}\widehat{Q}_{k}^{i}(b)},
which matches the expression in Algorithm 1 . Therefore, the distribution of y t k + 1 y_{t_{k+1}} defined by the CTMC matches the distribution of x t k + 1 x_{t_{k+1}} from the algorithm.
∎
F.4 Proof of Lemma 15
In view of the definition of D ⁡ ( ⋅ , ⋅ ) D(\cdot,\cdot) , one can write
s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ​ D ​ ( s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) , s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ) \displaystyle s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})D\left(s_{T-t}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell}),s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})\right)
= s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) − s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) + s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ​ log ⁡ s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) . \displaystyle=s_{T-t}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})+s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})\log\frac{s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})}{s_{T-t}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})}.
The first two terms cancel out in expectation by Lemma 14 ; i.e., for any c ∈ [ S ] c\in[S] , one has
𝔼 x t ∼ q ← t | ℓ ( ⋅ ∣ x ℓ ) [ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ( s T − t ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) − s T − t ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ) ] = 0 . \displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{t\mid\ell}(\cdot\mid x_{\ell})}\Big[\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\left(s_{T-t}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})-s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})\right)\Big]=0.
Next, using Equation 60 , we obtain
s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) s T − t ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c , x ℓ ) = q 0 ​ ( x t ⊙ i c ) ​ q 0 ​ ( x ℓ ) q 0 ​ ( x t ) ​ q 0 ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c ) . \frac{s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})}{s_{T-t}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c,x_{\ell})}=\frac{q_{0}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c)q_{0}(x_{\ell})}{q_{0}(x_{t})q_{0}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c)}.
Using this relation, we further bound
𝔼 x ℓ , x t ∼ q ← ℓ , t ​ ∑ i ∈ m ⁡ ( x t ) ∑ c ∈ [ S ] s T − t ​ ( x t ⊙ i c , x t ) ​ log ⁡ q 0 ​ ( x t ⊙ i c ) ​ q 0 ​ ( x ℓ ) q 0 ​ ( x t ) ​ q 0 ​ ( x ℓ ⊙ i c ) \displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{x_{\ell},x_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{\ell,t}}\sum_{i\in m(x_{t})}\sum_{c\in[S]}s_{T-t}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c,x_{t})\log\frac{q_{0}(x_{t}\odot_{i}c)q_{0}(x_{\ell})}{q_{0}(x_{t})q_{0}(x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c)}
= 𝔼 y ℓ , y t ∼ q ← ℓ , t ​ ∑ i ∉ m ⁡ ( y t ) log ⁡ q 0 ​ ( y t ) ​ q 0 ​ ( y ℓ ⊙ i MASK ) q 0 ​ ( y t ⊙ i MASK ) ​ q 0 ​ ( y ℓ ⊙ i y t i ) \displaystyle\quad={\mathbb{E}}_{y_{\ell},y_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{\ell,t}}\sum_{i\notin m(y_{t})}\log\frac{q_{0}(y_{t})q_{0}(y_{\ell}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK})}{q_{0}(y_{t}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK})q_{0}(y_{\ell}\odot_{i}y_{t}^{i})}
= ∑ i ∈ [ d ] 𝔼 y ℓ , y t ∼ q ← ℓ , t ​ log ⁡ q 0 ​ ( y t ) ​ q 0 ​ ( y ℓ ⊙ i MASK ) q 0 ​ ( y t ⊙ i MASK ) ​ q 0 ​ ( y ℓ ⊙ i y t i ) , \displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i\in[d]}{\mathbb{E}}_{y_{\ell},y_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{\ell,t}}\log\frac{q_{0}(y_{t})q_{0}(y_{\ell}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK})}{q_{0}(y_{t}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK})q_{0}(y_{\ell}\odot_{i}y_{t}^{i})},
(121)
where in the second line, we used the definition of the score function along with the natural bijection between the sets
{ ( x , i , c ) , for x ∈ 𝒳 , i ∈ m ( x ) , and c ∈ [ S ] } \{(x,i,c)\text{, for }x\in{\mathcal{X}}\text{, }i\in m(x)\text{, and }c\in[S]\} and { ( y , i ) , for y ∈ 𝒳 and i ∉ m ( y ) } \{(y,i)\text{, for }y\in{\mathcal{X}}\text{ and }i\notin m(y)\}
to change the measure under the expectation:
x t \displaystyle x_{t}
→ y t ⊙ i MASK \displaystyle\to y_{t}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK}
x ℓ \displaystyle x_{\ell}
→ y ℓ ⊙ i MASK \displaystyle\to y_{\ell}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK}
x t ⊙ i c \displaystyle x_{t}\odot_{i}c
→ y t \displaystyle\to y_{t}
x ℓ ⊙ i c \displaystyle x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c
→ y ℓ ⊙ i y t i . \displaystyle\to y_{\ell}\odot_{i}y_{t}^{i}.
Note that since y ℓ y_{\ell} appears earlier in the backward process, y ℓ i y_{\ell}^{i} can be masked or unmasked. Since the i i -th element of x ℓ ⊙ i c x_{\ell}\odot_{i}c is unmasked by construction, we explicitly set the i i -th element of y ℓ y_{\ell} to y t i y_{t}^{i} . The third line follows from the fact that, for i ∈ m ⁡ ( y t ) i\in m(y_{t}) , the term is equal to zero.
Next, we define, for fixed
t , y t t,y_{t} , and i ∈ [ d ] i\in[d] ,
f i ​ ( y ) ≔ log ⁡ q 0 ​ ( y ⊙ i y t i ) q 0 ​ ( y ⊙ i MASK ) . \displaystyle f_{i}(y)\coloneqq\log\frac{q_{0}(y\odot_{i}y_{t}^{i})}{q_{0}(y\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK})}.
To further control the right hand side of Equation 121 , we invoke Dynkin’s formula as described in Lemma 6 to obtain
∑ i ∈ [ d ] 𝔼 y ℓ , y t ∼ q ← ℓ , t ​ log ⁡ q 0 ​ ( y t ) ​ q 0 ​ ( y ℓ ⊙ i MASK ) q 0 ​ ( y t ⊙ i MASK ) ​ q 0 ​ ( y ℓ ⊙ i y t i ) \displaystyle\sum_{i\in[d]}{\mathbb{E}}_{y_{\ell},y_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{\ell,t}}\log\frac{q_{0}(y_{t})q_{0}(y_{\ell}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK})}{q_{0}(y_{t}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK})q_{0}(y_{\ell}\odot_{i}y_{t}^{i})}
= ∑ i ∈ [ d ] 𝔼 y ℓ , y t ∼ q ← ℓ , t ​ [ f i ​ ( y t ) − f i ​ ( y ℓ ) ] \displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i\in[d]}{\mathbb{E}}_{y_{\ell},y_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{\ell,t}}\left[f_{i}(y_{t})-f_{i}(y_{\ell})\right]
= ∑ i ∈ [ d ] ∫ ℓ t 𝔼 y v , y t ∼ q ← v , t ​ ∑ j ∉ m ⁡ ( y v ) ∪ { i } log ⁡ q 0 ( y v ⊙ i y t i ) q 0 ( y v ⊙ i MASK ⊙ j MASK ) q 0 ( y v ⊙ i MASK ) q 0 ( y v ⊙ i y t i ⊙ j MASK ) ​ 𝑑 v \displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i\in[d]}\int_{\ell}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{y_{v},y_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{v,t}}\sum_{j\notin m(y_{v})\cup\{i\}}\log\frac{q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{i}y_{t}^{i})q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK}\odot_{j}\mathrm{MASK})}{q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK})q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{i}y_{t}^{i}\odot_{j}\mathrm{MASK})}\mathrm{d}v
= ( i ) ∑ i ≠ j ∈ [ d ] ∫ ℓ t 𝔼 y v , y t ∼ q ← v , t ​ log ⁡ q 0 ( y v ⊙ i y t i ) q 0 ( y v ⊙ i MASK ⊙ j MASK ) q 0 ( y v ⊙ i MASK ) q 0 ( y v ⊙ i y t i ⊙ j MASK ) ​ 𝑑 v \displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{i})}}{{=}}\sum_{i\neq j\in[d]}\int_{\ell}^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{y_{v},y_{t}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{v,t}}\log\frac{q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{i}y_{t}^{i})q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK}\odot_{j}\mathrm{MASK})}{q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK})q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{i}y_{t}^{i}\odot_{j}\mathrm{MASK})}\mathrm{d}v
= ( ii ) ∑ i ≠ j ∈ [ d ] ∫ ℓ t e t − v ​ 𝔼 y v ∼ q ← v ​ log ⁡ q 0 ( y v ) q 0 ( y v ⊙ i MASK ⊙ j MASK ) q 0 ​ ( y v ⊙ i MASK ) ​ q 0 ​ ( y v ⊙ j MASK ) ​ 𝑑 v . \displaystyle\quad\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\text{ii})}}{{=}}\sum_{i\neq j\in[d]}\int_{\ell}^{t}e^{t-v}{\mathbb{E}}_{y_{v}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{v}}\log\frac{q_{0}(y_{v})q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK}\odot_{j}\mathrm{MASK})}{q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK})q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{j}\mathrm{MASK})}\mathrm{d}v.
(122)
Here for part ( i ) (\text{i}) , as before, we extended the sum to j ∈ m ⁡ ( y v ) ∖ { i } j\in m(y_{v})\setminus\{i\} since additional terms equal zero. As f i ​ ( y ) f_{i}(y) only depends on d − 1 d-1 coordinates of y y (all except the i i -th), the constraint j ≠ i j\neq i appears. Regarding part ( ii ) (\text{ii}) , it follows from
Pr ⁡ ( y v i ≠ MASK ∣ y t i ≠ MASK ) = e v − t . \displaystyle\Pr(y_{v}^{i}\neq\mathrm{MASK}\mid y_{t}^{i}\neq\mathrm{MASK})=e^{v-t}.
Next, let y v − ( i , j ) y_{v}^{-(i,j)} denote all unmasked elements of y v y_{v} , except i i -th and j j -th.
We can write
q 0 ( y v ) q 0 ( y v ⊙ i MASK ⊙ j MASK ) q 0 ​ ( y v ⊙ i MASK ) ​ q 0 ​ ( y v ⊙ j MASK ) = q 0 ​ ( y v i , y v j ∣ y v − ( i , j ) ) q 0 ​ ( y v i ∣ y v − ( i , j ) ) ​ q 0 ​ ( y v j ∣ y v − ( i , j ) ) , \displaystyle\frac{q_{0}(y_{v})q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK}\odot_{j}\mathrm{MASK})}{q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK})q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{j}\mathrm{MASK})}=\frac{q_{0}(y_{v}^{i},y_{v}^{j}\mid y_{v}^{-(i,j)})}{q_{0}(y_{v}^{i}\mid y_{v}^{-(i,j)})q_{0}(y_{v}^{j}\mid y_{v}^{-(i,j)})},
and thus,
∑ i ≠ j ∈ [ d ] ∫ ℓ t e t − v ​ 𝔼 y v ∼ q ← v ​ log ⁡ q 0 ( y v ) q 0 ( y v ⊙ i MASK ⊙ j MASK ) q 0 ​ ( y v ⊙ i MASK ) ​ q 0 ​ ( y v ⊙ j MASK ) ​ 𝑑 v \displaystyle\sum_{i\neq j\in[d]}\int_{\ell}^{t}e^{t-v}{\mathbb{E}}_{y_{v}\sim\overset{\leftarrow}{q}_{v}}\log\frac{q_{0}(y_{v})q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK}\odot_{j}\mathrm{MASK})}{q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{i}\mathrm{MASK})q_{0}(y_{v}\odot_{j}\mathrm{MASK})}\mathrm{d}v
= ∑ i ≠ j ∫ ℓ t e t − v ​ I ​ ( y v i ; y v j ∣ y v − ( i , j ) ) ​ 𝑑 v \displaystyle\quad=\sum_{i\neq j}\int_{\ell}^{t}e^{t-v}\mathrm{I}(y_{v}^{i};y_{v}^{j}\mid y_{v}^{-(i,j)})\mathrm{d}v
= ∫ ℓ t e t − v ​ ℐ ​ ( T − v ) ​ 𝑑 v , \displaystyle\quad=\int_{\ell}^{t}e^{t-v}{\mathcal{I}}(T-v)\mathrm{d}v,
(123)
as y v ∼ q T − v y_{v}\sim q_{T-v} . Combining Eqns. ( 121 ), ( 122 ), and ( 123 ) concludes the proof.
∎

may not have found yet. Your efforts will help us improve the HTML versions for all readers, because disability
should not be a barrier to accessing research. Thank you for your continued support in championing open access for
all.
